- (app shell review, 2026-10-14) arbor.log is capped at 2 MB with one older file kept. Arbor does not pause during fast user switching or display sleep. App Nap is held off with a ProcessInfo activity that still lets the Mac sleep.
- Input review, 2026-10-14: shortcuts do not repeat while held. Carbon sends one press however long the key is down (checked on a Mac), and a camera that runs on while a key is held would overshoot.
- Input review, 2026-10-14: a pinch without ⌥ over a menu, the menu bar, a floating palette or anything else above the ordinary windows is the app's, not Arbor's (`PointerSurface`).
- Input review, 2026-10-14: an event tap that macOS switches off three times in ten seconds stays off for thirty (`TapHealth`).
- F05 follow-up, 2026-10-14: the Desktop's modifier is ⇧ alone and fixed; `frameRectangleOnDesktop` only turns it on and off. ⇧ with any other modifier stays with the app.
- F05 follow-up, 2026-10-14: ⇧-drag that starts on a Desktop icon, or on the Dock, draws a rectangle too, because the window list cannot tell them from the wallpaper and asking Finder would hold every ⇧-click up.
- F16 input, 2026-10-14: a scroll is Arbor's only if ⌥ is held as it begins or a pinch of Arbor's is in progress. Not on the bare Desktop without ⌥: finding that out would cost every scroll on the Mac a look at the window list.
- F16 input, 2026-10-14: the momentum after a scroll of Arbor's is swallowed and not passed on; the gesture ends when the fingers lift, which is when the view settles.
- F16 input, 2026-10-14: ⌥ + a wheel mouse is left to the app: clicks of a wheel have no beginning and no end.
- F16 input, 2026-10-14: `continuousZoom` is false until the model ships, and until then `Controller.gesture(_:)` counts a streamed pinch into F03's steps.
- F10 (Q8 h, nobody has seen TrellisWM's item): from left to right back, forward, the Desktop, Show Everything, then one digit per **saved** view in slot order; unsaved slots get no button. Glyphs: SF Symbols `chevron.left`, `chevron.right`, `menubar.dock.rectangle`, Arbor's own mark for Show Everything, digits. 22-point cells, 4 points more between the three groups.
- F10: "filled in" is a rounded rectangle behind the glyph at 25% opacity (`menuBarFill`; TrellisWM's default is 0.12, which was faint on a real menu bar). Disabled glyphs are drawn at 35%. Several buttons are filled at once when they name the same space.
- F10: the buttons describe the display under the pointer, like the commands they run. Nothing reports the pointer changing display and Arbor does not poll, so the item follows at the next change, and after every click on it.
- F10: a click in the padding around the image or in a gap between groups counts for the nearest button; a click on a disabled button does nothing; a right click, ⌃-click, ⌥-click, or a press that is no mouse click (VoiceOver) opens the menu.
- F10: back and forward are enabled only when they would move the view; `state`'s `canGoBack` and `canGoForward` now say the same (history can hold places that have become where the view already is).
- F10: a saved view's tooltip and menu title say what it holds in the words of Arbor's replies, without the node number ("View 2: Safari and Mail").
- F10: the menu follows `docs/MENU.md` (the product owner's): states first, then View, Window, Arrangement, Arbor, with native right-aligned shortcuts, a tick on the place the view is at, and Saved Views as a submenu with Go To and Save This View As. "Float All Windows", as in the glossary, not TrellisWM's "…on Desktop".
- F10: the welcome window never activates Arbor (a non-activating panel), because "the focused window" is the active app's, and ⌃⌥↩ on its second page must frame the user's window, not find Arbor's. Its second page floats above other windows; its first does not, so that System Settings can come in front.
- F10: Check Setup…, a launch without the permission and opening Arbor again open the window on how things stand (first page); Welcome… opens it on the three moves; it turns to them by itself when the permission arrives while it is open, unless a row of the first page still advises something (changed 2026-10-14: the first move is a drag to a screen edge, which macOS as it comes takes for itself, and whoever comes back from granting the permission has read no other row); either way it comes in front of System Settings then. `ARBOR_NO_PROMPT` stops it from opening by itself, not the commands `check-setup` and `welcome`.
- F10, F46: the three moves tick in whatever order they are made and stay ticked; what is already so when the window opens is ticked at once; Show Everything counts on a display whose view has been somewhere else since the window opened.
- F10: a row that is in order shows its tick and no text. With more rows than the display is tall, the rows scroll.
- F10, F42: `doctor` fails (and `arborctl doctor` exits with 1) when a check found a problem. Problems: Accessibility missing, a Desktop & Dock setting in the way, a competing app running, VoiceOver on while shortcuts are, a gesture's tap missing, a shortcut refused, a skipped settings entry, no display. Only notes: Screen Recording missing, shared Spaces, several input sources, a pause. What cannot be read is "unknown here", never a problem.
- F10, F42: several input sources are a note, not a problem. Measured on macOS 26 with a second layout enabled: ⌃⌥Space still reached Arbor and the input source did not change.
- F10: `check-setup`, `welcome`, `doctor` and `menu-bar` are not accepted from `arbor://` URLs: none moves the view or arranges windows.
- F10: the icon is a rounded square with a green gradient (sRGB 0.18 0.63 0.38 to 0.04 0.29 0.20) and the mark in white, the cells further out fainter; below 64 pixels the two leaves become one.
- F10 (after the product owner's round): while nothing is docked on the display under the pointer the item is Arbor's mark alone and a click opens the menu, because a row of buttons with nowhere to go takes 92 points for nothing on day one. With buttons, the last one (`ellipsis`) opens the menu with a plain click, for those who would never right-click.
- F10: a permission taken away while Arbor runs is F63's alone; F10's own version (a forced pause and a count of windows left out of view) is gone. F10 shows what F63 knows: the menu's state lines, the icon, `doctor` and the welcome window's first page (`OnboardingModel(windowsOutOfView:)` is ready for its count).
- F10: an item of the menu that would fail is greyed out: Step Out and Step In with nowhere to go, Dock → Left for a window already at the view's left edge or filling the view, every row about the focused window when there is none Arbor arranges, Arrange Windows without windows. The four ways back are the exception (`docs/MENU.md`, rule 1).
- F10: "Float Focused Window" runs `toggle-float`, which is what ⌃⌥F runs, so that the row shows the shortcut; it is greyed out on a window that floats, where that command would dock it.
- F10: with no focused window the row about its app reads "Don’t Arrange This App", greyed out, so that the rows below it stay where they are.
- F10: Arrange Again lists apps by the name of an open window's app, else by what Finder calls the installed app, else by the last part of the bundle id.
- F10: not built from `docs/MENU.md`: the two rows that change while ⌥ is held (Show Everything on All Displays, Pause for an Hour). AppKit pairs alternates by key equivalent, and these rows show different ones; it needs a Mac to try.
- F10: `doctor` reports as refused only the shortcuts macOS refused when the app last asked, instead of comparing what is registered with what the settings describe.
- F10: opening Arbor again while the welcome window is open brings it forward on the page it shows; closed, it opens on how things stand.
- F12 window: three levels. `normal` is in the window, `advanced` behind a disclosure in its section, `hidden` not in the window; the level changes nothing about `set`, `get`, `reset`, the file, `settings` or docs/SETTINGS.md. A section whose entries are all advanced shows them at once.
- F12 window: a search finds advanced settings (whoever searches for one wants to see it), never hidden ones; shortcuts and rules are found as panes to go to.
- F12 window: Restore Defaults restores what the section can show, so not its hidden keys, which nobody looking at the window knows about.
- F12 window: a slider changes the setting when it is let go of, not on the way (every change moves real windows and writes a file); moved without a drag (arrow keys, VoiceOver) it changes it at once. Fields commit on Return, on losing the keyboard, and when the window closes.
- F12 window: recording binds the new keys first and unbinds the old ones only if that took. Keys another command has are taken only after "Use It Here Instead". Escape alone cancels, so Escape alone cannot be recorded.
- F12 window: a new rule starts as `dock right` and is adjusted on its line.
- F12 window: the hidden main menu that makes ⌘W, ⌘C, ⌘V, ⌘A and ⌘Z work has no Quit: ⌘Q pressed to be rid of a settings window must not end the window manager.
- F12 window: it floats while it has the keyboard (tiles and gap handles are drawn again, in front, after every change) and is an ordinary window otherwise; it moves to the macOS Space it is asked for on; closing it gives the keyboard back to the app that was in front before.
- F12 window: ⌃⌥, is handed back during a pause like every shortcut that does not end it; the paused menu, and the one that waits for Accessibility, have Settings….
- F12: bundle ids in rules are compared ignoring case (no two apps differ by that alone). `rule add` and `rule change` turn the name, or the mis-cased id, of an app with a window open into its bundle id, and refuse any other word without a dot.
- F12: `bind` allows every shortcut it allowed before and adds a caution to the reply (and the window to the line) for one without ⌃⌥ ("no app gets ⌘Q while Arbor runs") and for the three ⌃⌥⌘ chords macOS uses for Accessibility.
- F12: a shortcut can be written as menus show it (`⌃⌥Space`, `⌃⌥⇧←`); every shortcut's label parses back to it.
- F12: `ruleTitleWait` and `continuousZoom` are hidden (the first by the product owner's table, the second until F16's model ships).
- F12: what the window shows takes its words from docs/GLOSSARY.md, and a test (`whatTheWindowShowsIsInTheGlossarysWords`) knows the commonest wrong ones. It leaves hidden entries alone, so an entry whose words, section or level are in doubt can land as `hidden: true` and be placed later.
- F12 window: the lists of apps have a section of their own, Apps, in the schema, the document and the window, each under its own label ("Don't arrange" first), advanced ones behind the disclosure. `settings-window dont-arrange` opens it: a list names the pane it is in, by label or key. Restore Defaults leaves lists alone, as `reset all` does.
- F12: `set <list> add <app>` and `set <list> remove <app>` change any list of apps (the product owner: one verb for changing a setting, no top-level `add` and `remove`); the list goes by key or by label (`set dont-arrange add Music`); `set excludedBundleIDs add x` does what `exclude x` does, and unlike it works during a pause and before Accessibility is granted. An app is on a list whatever the case of its bundle id; it can be named as in `rule add`; what is on a list can be taken off whatever it looks like.
- F12: `set <key> toggle` flips a switch and is refused for anything else.
- F37 (product owner): no focus ring while nothing is docked on any display, so that launching Arbor, and Float All Windows, leave the Mac looking like a Mac. Six of the ring's tests, which used only floating windows, now dock one window first; what each checks is unchanged.
- Review: replies name only what can be seen in a space: minimized windows, hidden apps, windows on other macOS Spaces and places kept for windows that do not exist are left out.
- Coordinator, 2026-10-14, on the product owner's open points (`collab/reports/2026-10-14-product-owner-round-1.md`): **F09** a docked window let go before the hold is over goes back to its slot, a floating one stays where it is. **F14** ships `desktopDoubleClick` false until F60 lands, shares the trunk's un-frame rule, drops `tileClick` and hides `titleBarHeight`. **F04** ships `windowButtonsActOnGroup` false unless the whole tab group really minimizes; `tab close` fails outside a tab group and is never on the URL list. **`gapHandles`** is one choice key, `shown`, `hidden` or `off`. **`blurBehindArranged`** runs from 0 to 1. The update setting is **`automaticUpdateChecks`**. **`BuildInfo`** is the one version format. **Undo** acts on the focused window's display (F56's rule).
- Compat lab: a docked window that comes out up to 36 points larger than its slot is taken for one that sizes in steps (Terminal rounds to the nearest character cell) and is asked for less, up to four times, before its size is learned as a minimum. 36 is a cell at a very large font; nobody has observed what TrellisWM does.
- F45: the ten minutes are the setting `tileRetryMinutes`; 0 or less never asks, and `relayout` always does.
- F45: there is no timer. A window is asked again at the first layout after the interval, so an idle Mac stays still, and a tile can outlast the ten minutes until something is laid out (a camera move, a window opening or closing, a resize).
- F45: nobody is asked during a drag or an edge resize, so that drop targets do not change under the pointer; the layout that ends the drag asks.
- F07 (product owner, 2026-10-15; found by a random walk): a docked window's edge pulled **by hand** by two to four points was neither followed nor put back: the window stayed out of its slot. Four points are what passes for an app rounding its size, so no gap follows; but between "a resize began" and "it ended" Arbor knows that it was a hand, and puts the window back, in one move. The same change without a hand is still the app's rounding and is accepted. One point is in place to every layout.
- F45: a refusal replaces the record with the size just seen, in the dimensions that were refused, and starts the interval again.
- F45: seeing a window smaller than its record lowers the record to what was seen (#17) rather than forgetting it; the effect in criterion 3 is the same, and a true minimum that was merely overestimated stays known.
- F61: nobody in view loses their place to a newcomer. Room is what the Desktop and the neighbours can spare; a window that needs more than that is the tile, even though it was docked last.
- F61, second round (product owner, 12:21 to 13:27 on 2026-10-14): docked windows come before the Desktop, always, and none becomes a tile for its sake; room goes back to whom it was taken from; the window with the keyboard is the last to become a tile. This overrules the next line and the 240 points of a Desktop in use.
- F61 (product owner, review of #94): what Arbor took from the Desktop it gives back while a window floats on it, at the next move of the view or change of the arrangement; the window docked first pays with its place if nobody can spare the room. Who is served first is decided by the order of docking alone, no longer by who had the keyboard.
- F61: an empty Desktop and a window that is a tile give down to 80 points, a Desktop with a floating window on it down to 240 × 160. A Desktop that Arbor squeezed into a tile is topped up to 240 from what its neighbours can spare; one that a person or `arrangeDesktopWeight` made narrow is left alone.
- F61: a slot squeezed into a tile by hand (gap, window edge, `resize`) stays as it is through every later layout, until the same hand widens it or the window is given another place. Not saved across a restart.
- F61: scenario 02 (tiles) and ten tests told their story with a window of 900 × 600 or so, for which Arbor now finds room. They tell it with more than anybody can spare; nothing else about them changed. Two of F45's tests run with `makeRoom` off.
- F55: ⌃⌥ + scroll follows the direction macOS reports (after "natural scrolling"): what scrolls a page up steps in. One notch of a wheel is one step, whatever acceleration makes of it.
- F55: `scrollZoom` is on by default; the scroll event tap is installed but paused, and switched on only while ⌃⌥ is down or a scroll or pinch of Arbor's is in progress, so ordinary scrolling never passes through Arbor. `scrollOnDesktopZooms` stays off: people scroll by accident, and it costs a window list per scroll.
- F55: with ⌃⌥ held a scroll up or down zooms and sideways it slides (F16; the product owner's decision further down); ⌥ alone is the app's; with `continuousZoom`, 40 points of ⌃⌥-scroll move the view as far as 0.25 of a pinch.
- F56, Q2, Q12: `stepInToward` has three values and `"auto"` is the default: a shortcut follows the focused window, unless the pointer has been used since the last key was released and since the focus last changed. The one key decides both the display a shortcut acts on and what `in` steps toward.
- F56: `"pointer"` and `"focus"` bind shortcuts, `arborctl` and URLs only. A pinch always means what is under the pointer; the menu always means the display whose menu bar was clicked.
- F56: shortcuts pressed one after another with nothing typed in between stay on the display the first one chose, however long the pause: ⌃⌥↑ ⌃⌥↓ must never act on two displays.
- F56: `in` from the menu steps toward the focused window (the pointer is resting on the menu item), unless `stepInToward` is `"pointer"`.
- F56: `in` toward a focused window that floats steps toward the Desktop. A focused window that Arbor does not arrange leaves the choice to the pointer.
- F56: using the pointer means moving, clicking, dragging or scrolling. Arbor never sees what is typed: it asks macOS how long ago a key was last released and how many were pressed.
- F56: `arrange`, `float-all here`, `save-view` and `zoom-node` choose their display as the camera commands do (this replaces "`arrange` stays with the display under the pointer" and the controller review's "camera commands keep acting on the display under the pointer, also from the keyboard" above). A command that fails with several displays says which one it chose: "on the focused window's display" or "on the display under the pointer".
- F51: during a peek the arrow keys and ⎋ are ⌃⌥ + arrow and ⌃⌥⎋ (the modifiers are being held), so for that long they do not step, go to the neighbour or pause. They keep their usual meaning for the first 0.3 s after the press and get it back the moment the peek ends.
- F51: only the shortcut bound to `show-all` peeks, whatever its keys. `home` (⌃⌥0) never does, so there is always a Show Everything that cannot spring back.
- F51: the 0.3 s start when everything has been laid out, not when the key goes down, so a tap is a tap however slow the windows are. When Arbor cannot tell whether the keys are still down, the peek ends as a tap.
- F51: the outline starts on the view that was left and ignores a pointer at rest (4 points of slop); with everything already showing nothing is outlined, and the first arrow starts from the focused window.
- F51: the Desktop and tiles can be outlined and landed on like windows. Over a gap or the menu bar the outline stays where it was. The pointer on another display chooses nothing.
- F51: a click lands when the button comes up on the space it went down on. It does not reach the app: with ⌃⌥ held it would be a ⌃-click (contextual menu) and an ⌥-click (macOS hides the app that had the keyboard).
- F51: another command, a drag or an edge resize during the hold leaves the view on everything with one history entry, as a tap does, and letting go then does nothing: nothing may jump under what the user has just done.
- F51: the outlined window closing does not end the peek; the outline goes back to the view that was left. If that is gone as well, letting go stays on everything.
- F51: landing gives the keyboard to the framed window (in a group: the window under the pointer, otherwise the first), unless a window in the framed space already has it.
- F51 (words): "peek" and "outline" join the glossary. The focus ring is a ring; what a peek draws is an outline.
- F41: in an app on `passThroughApps` Arbor keeps `toggle-pause` (⌃⌥⎋) and any shortcut given to `pass-through`, and nothing else; the spec said "no shortcuts". One key must answer everywhere, and a toggle's key must survive what it switches on. `"ctrl+opt+escape": null` gives that up too.
- F41: no app is on either list by default, not even remote desktops and virtual machines: someone who frames one still wants ⌃⌥Space to get out. `bindings` suggests `pass-through` for known apps that have a window open (`ControlOptionApps`); F42's check can use the same list.
- F41: full screen changes nothing by itself; the frontmost app decides, on whichever macOS Space it is.
- F41: bundle ids on the two lists are compared whole and without regard to case; no wildcards.
- F41: a pause for an app is ended only by that app leaving the front. `pause` by hand during it makes it a pause by hand; `resume` by hand inside the app holds until an app on the list next comes to the front; a timed pause that runs out inside such an app goes on as the app's; an app on both lists pauses.
- F41: shortcuts are unregistered rather than kept and passed on: only then does the window server deliver the hardware's own events, which is what virtual machines and remote desktops read. (Costs and the `postToPid` experiment: `scripts/mac-shell/check-F41.sh`, still to be run.)
- F44: a shortcut's letter, digit or sign is the key that types it **without modifiers**; the table macOS uses while ⌘ is held ("Dvorak – QWERTY ⌘") is not consulted, because Arbor's letter shortcuts do not hold ⌘.
- F44: after falling back to its US position a key is shown as what is printed on it (⌃⌥Ü), or as the wanted character if the key types that with ⇧ (French digits stay 1–9).
- F44: of two shortcuts that land on one key the first in the list wins (the user's own come first) and the other is reported by `bindings` and in the log, naming the layout.
- F44: `us:` before a key's name means its position on a US keyboard (`ctrl+opt+us:h`); it is another shortcut than `ctrl+opt+h`, so each can be bound, removed and replaced on its own.
- F44: `bindings` keeps printing shortcuts as settings.json spells them and adds the printed key in brackets only where it differs.
- F63: when Accessibility permission goes, Arbor moves nothing (it could not, and a false alarm then shows nothing), saves, removes its overlays and taps, and stops. One "no" out of the blue is asked about again 0.5 s later; one "no" before a command, from the taps' watchdog or with a refused call is believed at once.
- F63: with windows out of view the ways home (`show-all`, `home`, `float-all`, `pause`, `toggle-pause`) stay registered; they beep and open the Accessibility pane, at most once in ten seconds. With nothing out of view no shortcut is held and nothing appears but the warning sign: whoever switched Arbor off on purpose is not nagged.
- F63: when the permission returns Arbor starts as at launch and, if windows had been out of view, shows everything on every display; Back returns to the old view. "They come back" has to be seen to be true. Not after a loss of under three seconds (a false alarm, a slip of the finger): the view then stays as it was.
- F63: a pause of any kind ends with the loss and does not return with the permission: granting means wanting Arbor, as launching does. An app on `pauseForApps` that is still frontmost gets a new pause.
- F63: no dialog before quitting without the permission; the menu item reads "Quit Arbor (3 windows stay out of view)".
- F40, F41 (product owner): a resume always ends with the focused window in view, whoever began the pause.
- F41 (product owner): replies, `status`, `bindings` and the menu say "shortcuts are off in Emacs" and "paused for Game"; `pass-through` takes `on` or `off` as a last word and otherwise toggles; the menu's first line in a listed app says that the shortcuts are off there.
- F44 (product owner): a sign with ⇧ resolves as the key that types, with ⇧, what ⇧ makes of that sign on a US keyboard (⌃⌥⇧/ is ⌃⌥⇧ß in Germany), falling back to the ordinary rules; digits with ⇧ stay digits.
- F44 (product owner): any single character the layout types alone can name its key (`ctrl+opt+ü`); such an entry, like `us:[`, also replaces or removes the default that sits on that key on the layout in use.
- F56 (product owner's note on #33): a focused window that Arbor does not arrange still says which DISPLAY a shortcut acts on, the one it sits on; this replaces "leaves the choice to the pointer" above for the display. `in` has nothing to step toward there and chooses as before. A value of `stepInToward` nobody defined is logged once and means `auto`.
- F49: Arbor looks whether a pinned window is covered, and raises it (never focuses it) if it is, after every camera move, after every layout that is not a live mouse drag, and whenever a window that is not pinned takes the keyboard. The spec asked for camera moves only; a click on the window under the call would bury it.
- F49: it looks three times: when the command or event is over (commands focus a window as their last step), and 0.2 s and 0.4 s later, not counting looks made while the mouse button is down, because on macOS 26 a clicked window takes the keyboard first and comes to the front afterwards (seen by the focus ring, F37). A pinned window that no window Arbor knows overlaps from above is not raised: raising asks its app, which may be busy with a call. The stacking comes from the window server (`windowsFrontToBack`, as in F02).
- F49: a focus report for a pinned window never leads to raising, so two pinned windows of one app cannot set each other off; while a pinned window has the keyboard nothing is raised over it; among themselves pinned windows keep the order they were last used in.
- F49: a camera move never gives a pinned window the keyboard, even when it is the only window in view (keys typed into a call by surprise mute and hang up). With nothing else in view the keyboard stays where it was, as it does on an empty Desktop today.
- F49: dragging a pinned window by its title bar only moves it: no preview, no docking, not even at a screen edge. It lives over docked windows, so nearly every nudge would otherwise dock it. `dock` and ⌃⌥⌘ + arrow dock it and end the pin.
- F49: `arrange` and `arrange all` leave pinned windows out. `float-all`, `pause` and quitting do not touch them.
- F49: `unpin` moves nothing: the window stays until the camera next moves, like a new window.
- F49: pinning a docked window puts it where `float` would, but one that would cover more than half of the display takes at most half its width and height, centred on its old slot. F48 will make this rare. **Overruled by Q20 on 2026-10-14:** it takes its own size, however large.
- F49: pinning a floating window that is out of view brings it back to where it was last left, fitted into the display; the camera does not move.
- F49: a pinned window may hang over a screen edge if the user puts it there; only when less than half of it is on any display (a display changed shape) is it brought back.
- F49: a display is unplugged: its pinned windows come into view on the main display, still pinned, and return to their exact frames when it comes back. Unpinned meanwhile, they stay unpinned. A window pinned meanwhile stays where it was pinned instead of returning to its old slot, like a window docked meanwhile.
- F49: at launch a pinned window whose number is gone is matched by app and title, like docked windows (other floating windows are not matched). It stays where it is on screen.
- F49: words: the menu says Keep “Zoom” in View; commands, the rule action and `state` say `pin`; replies say "keeping w12 in view". Added to `docs/GLOSSARY.md`. ⌃⌥⇧D is reserved for `toggle-pin` in `docs/KEYBOARD.md`, unbound.
- F49: arbor-sim now runs work scheduled "at once" after every line, as the app's run loop does, so scenarios see pinned windows raised without `flush`. New `expect` forms: `pinned <id>`, `in-front <id>`.
- F49: a `pin` rule can take over when a window's title arrives late (`ruleTitleWait`): a window an earlier rule docked goes back to where its app opened it and is pinned; a late `float` rule takes an earlier rule's pin away.
- F49, product owner's verdict (2026-10-14): never docking by a drag, `arrange` leaving it out, no keyboard from a move of the view (it keeps the keyboard if it had it) and raising after a click beneath are accepted; the last needs a real Mac before anyone relies on it.
- F49: a pin follows macOS's own window tabs (each tab is a window with its own number, F17); pinning a window that opened over a framed one (a guest, F57) ends the visit: it belongs to the display, and unpinned it is an ordinary floating window.
- F49, product owner's notes on #40 (2026-10-14), all taken: (1) a command that moves the view takes the keyboard to the window that arrives, **also out of a pinned window** (this reverses "it keeps the keyboard if it had it"); a command that leaves the view where it is leaves the keyboard alone, and on an empty Desktop it stays. (2) `zoom` without an id in a pinned window un-frames what is beneath; with nothing framed, and with the id, it fails: "w103 is kept in view; dock it to give it a place". (3) Docking says "and it is no longer kept in view". (4) README's bullet, guide, release notes, glossary and help tag say "keeps its place", not "in front", until `check-F49.sh` has run. (5) `pin` records the frame the window has as its floating frame, so F48's float-all and quit do not move one that had been pushed aside (B6's finding too). (6) `pin 103 104` is a usage error; the help line no longer says "floating"; `status` says "(1 kept in view)". Left as it is: `exclude` then `include` drops the pin, as it drops a docked window's place.
- F24 (watch agent, 2026-10-14): events report how the state differs from the last report, once per turn of the main loop, not what was done; a change undone within the same turn is never mentioned.
- F24: the names are the glossary's, as the product owner asked before protocol 1 froze: event `view` (not camera), kind `everything` (not root), `savedView`, `outOfView`, states `in-view`, `out-of-view`, `tile`, `minimized`, `hidden`, `full-screen`, `other-space`. `node` stays, because `zoom-node` takes it.
- F24: `hello`, `paused`, `resumed` and `quitting` are sent whatever the filter: whether Arbor is there at all is news for every client.
- F24: `quitting` comes only from `EventFeed.stop()`, which the app calls when it quits. That the controller stopped says nothing: a pause stops it too.
- F24: a pause is `paused`, and `arborctl watch` stays connected; after `resumed`, as after `started`, the whole state follows.
- F24: `opened` and `closed` are only for windows that really opened or closed while Arbor was looking. A window that was there already is `adopted` (after `started` and `resumed`, or when its app is arranged again); one that is still there is `released` (Don't Arrange).
- F24: one `window` line per window and change (flat, easy for `jq` and `grep`), each with all the window's fields as they are now; a window a rule docks at once is `opened` then `docked`; a window that changes display is `docked` or `floated` again, with the new display; a change of `state` is `"change":"state"`.
- F24 (product owner, before protocol 1 froze): the event that carries one window is `window`, as `view` and `layout` carry one display's; `displays` and the lists in `hello.snapshot` stay plural. `minimized` with a z, as `state` and Accessibility spell it: prose is British, names in a protocol are names. New values of `state`, `change` and `kind` may appear without `protocol` going up.
- F24 (product owner, laid beside F50's queries): a display's UUID is `displayID` in every event and in `hello.snapshot`; `display` is kept for the display's number (1 is the main one), which commands take and which may be added to the events later. `full-screen` is a state of its own, not `other-space`. The values, the same in events and queries: `in-view`, `out-of-view`, `tile`, `minimized`, `hidden`, `full-screen`, `other-space`.
- F24 (product owner, third list, the last before protocol 1 froze): a key means one thing in every line. Counts are `dockedCount`, `floatingCount`, `tileCount` (`docked` is true or false); a new window title is `"change":"title"` (with named spaces `renamed` would be read as the space's); `hello` carries `snapshot` (`state` is a word in `window`); the last line is `quitting`, as the reply to `quit` says. `stopped`, with a `reason`, is kept for Arbor ceasing to arrange without quitting or pausing, and comes with F63. Event lines refer (`window`, `displayID`); they never are, so no `id` in `hello`'s lists.
- F24: after `started` and `resumed` a docked window still gets two lines, `adopted` then `docked`, although `adopted` carries `"docked":true`: a script that follows only `docked` and `floated` stays right without knowing the other changes.
- F24: `view.label` is `Controller+Words.name(of:on:)` without the number in brackets; `view.depth` is how often `out` can be run; `view.outOfView` counts the display's windows whose state is `out-of-view`.
- F24 (product owner): `view.name`, the name a person gave a space (F20), is sent to every watcher, with or without `--titles`, and never logged: it was typed to be shown and the socket is the user's own; titles are different because the user did not write them and cannot foresee them. `label` stays Arbor's own words ("Safari and Mail") for a named space too.
- F24: while the controller has a layout put off (macOS reports a switch of its Spaces window by window; a window is being carried to another Space) nothing is sent; the feed looks again every 0.05 s. A space of which nothing can be seen just now is labelled by the apps it holds, never by its number alone.
- F24: `view.windows` lists the docked windows in view only (not windows tiles stand in for, not floating ones); `view.node` is the space that really fills the display, which is wider than the camera's own node while that has nothing to show.
- F24: with nothing docked the Desktop is the whole arrangement, and `kind` is `everything`.
- F24: `canGoBack` and `canGoForward` say whether the command would do anything (as F10's `Workspace.canGoBack` does), not whether the history is non-empty.
- F24: `settings` names the keys of settings.json that changed; a reload that changes none sends nothing.
- F24: `focus` is who has the keyboard according to the system, asked after every report that may have moved it and once more 0.25 s later, not whichever window reported last (an app in the background can report too).
- F24: rectangles in events are objects (`{"x","y","width","height"}`), as F50 plans for `state`, which still has `[[x,y],[w,h]]`.
- F24: `event` is the first key of every line and the rest are sorted, so `grep` and shell patterns work without `jq`; fields with nothing to say are left out, not null.
- F24: `arborctl watch` exits with 0 after `quitting` or when its own reader leaves, 1 when refused, 2 when Arbor is not running, 3 when the events end without `quitting`.
- F24: `arborctl` prints a refusal to standard error (F50 part 4), and `--json` exits with 1 on a refusal too, with the whole reply on standard output.
- F24: limits: 16 watchers, 256 KB behind, 64 connections, a backlog of 128, requests of 64 KB, 2 s to send a request, 5 s to collect a reply.
- F24: a watcher that closes its writing side counts as gone (that is how a client that has left is noticed without writing to it).
- F24: `watch` from somewhere that cannot keep listening (a shortcut, a URL) fails in words.
- F24: `hello.version` is F13's `BuildInfo.version` ("0.9.0", or "dev" outside a bundle), read from the controller when a client connects; there is no version constant.
- Core review (T60): a damaged `state.json` is repaired rather than refused wherever the child lists still make a tree; windows that lose their place float. Only a missing root, more than 10,000 nodes or more than 100 levels of nesting means a fresh start for that display, and the other displays are kept.
- Core review: a gap too large for its room gives way instead of squeezing windows to nothing. The outer gap takes at most a quarter of the display's shorter side on each side, and the gaps of a row or column at most half of it. Gaps are rounded to whole points.
- Core review (F01): moving a window toward several equally near neighbours picks the one that shares most of the edge, then the topmost or leftmost. Framing a rectangle that fits a split and its only visible child equally frames the child.
- Core review (T16): `arrange` fills columns that differ by at most one window, the taller ones first (7 windows: 3, 2, 2 instead of 3, 3, 1).
- F62: a group is kept while the view or a saved view is on it, while it has a name, and while it is a guest. History keeps nothing: a place visited long ago must not decide how a drag or a resize behaves today.
- F62: a group kept for a view still gives way to its last window, as before; one kept for a name or as a guest stays with one window, so that what is docked beside that window is in the group. It goes with its last window, and the name with it.
- F62: a group the view has left lasts until the next change to the arrangement (so Back finds it), then dissolves as it always did.
- F62 (T01): what is docked at an edge of the view joins what is in view, so the view shows what it showed and the newcomer, instead of widening to the whole row. The only window in view cannot be moved to an edge of the view.
- F10: the item's buttons and the menu's rows are about the display under the pointer, read without `commandDisplayID()`: a command from the menu follows the pointer (F56), and drawing is no command.
- F10: with no focused window the rows that name it read "Keep This Window in View", "Don’t Arrange This App" and "Shortcuts Off in This App", greyed out.
- F20: a name is 1 to 40 characters in any script; capitals are kept and ignored when comparing; runs of spaces, and `/` (which separates words in commands and URLs), count as one space. Control and invisible formatting characters are refused, the emoji joiner excepted.
- F20: every word `go` reads as a direction is refused as a whole name, not only the six of notice 4: also `l`, `r`, `u`, `d`, `north`, `south`, `east`, `west`. Otherwise `go d` would stop meaning down the day a space is named "d".
- F20: `go` prefers a whole name on any display to a beginning on the acting one. Of the same name on two other displays, the one macOS lists first wins; the other is reached from its own display.
- F20: `go` to a space on another display also gives a window there the keyboard (the one that had it last, else the top-left one), so that F56's rule makes the next shortcut act there. The pointer is not moved.
- F20: going where the view already is succeeds. `ask-name cancel` with no field open succeeds too. Scripts need not ask first.
- F20: `unname <text>` needs the whole name; only `go` guesses from a beginning.
- F20: **when the last window of a named space closes, floats or moves to another display, the name goes.** It is not handed to the group around it, and no empty space is kept. Undo brings it back with the arrangement.
- F20: a name whose space holds a single window follows that window when it is docked elsewhere, also when the space is a group that is down to it. If the window becomes a tab in a place without a name, the tab group has the name (better than losing it); if that place has a name, that one stays.
- F20: a window docked at an edge of the view while a named space fills it joins the space: F62 groups it with what was in view, and the name moves to that group. Saved views on the old group stay on the old group.
- F20: the only window of a named group cannot be moved with the keyboard inside its view ("fills the view; step out first"), as a framed window cannot.
- F20: naming is not an undo step, like saving a view. `arrange` and `float-all` remove names as they do saved views.
- F20: from URLs only `go` is accepted. `name` would let a page put its words into the menu and the replies, `unname` deletes, `ask-name` opens a field that takes the keyboard.
- F20 (the product owner's decision, on #107): names are not logged, like window titles; the log gets `‹name›` and the space's number. Done where the line is logged, by taking out whatever reads like a name that exists, existed before the command or is in the command: a space named "Mail" costs the log the app's name as well, too much rather than too little. A refused URL that parsed is logged as its command without the name; one that did not parse is logged as it came.
- F20 (the product owner's decision): `arrange` and `float-all` say how many names went ("arranged 4 windows; 2 names are gone").
- F20: the field closes when the view leaves its space rather than following it or naming a space that is no longer in view. While it is open, `name` and `unname` act on its space even if the pointer has gone to another display.
- F20: the menu follows docs/MENU.md, not the brief's "Spaces" submenu: names are rows of Saved Views, after the numbers, then Name This Space…. There is no row to remove a name: an empty field does that.
- F20: the field also closes when another window really gets the keyboard, decided in the controller from the focus reports, so that it does not depend on what AppKit tells a panel of an app that is never active. A report from an app in the background does not count.
- F20: lists are in alphabetical order, the acting display's names first. A name that another display shadows is listed by `spaces` and left out of the menu, whose rows run `go <name>`.
- F20, open: should a name be carried into F54's notation and so into presets (`(Mail / Notes)=Post`)? Not done; `Workspace.setName` after `reset(tree:)` is all it would take (B9, B12).
- F20, open: F52 turns an unplugged display into a guest space on another display. Its names stay with the dormant workspace today; they could come along, prefixed or not (B5).
- Performance review: no caches for `presentWindowIDs`, `workspaceID(containing:)` or `managedWindowIDs`. They cost 1 to 10 µs at 150 windows, a thousandth of one Accessibility call; timed tests catch it if that changes.
- Performance review: `stats` is a query, always on, and refused from `arbor://` URLs like every new command. It counts commands that failed as well: they took time too.
- Performance review: the state file is written only when the state differs from what was written last, on a queue of its own; quitting and pausing wait for it.
- Performance review: no bracket around the reports of an app that hides, shows or quits (`reportsBegan` / `reportsEnded`, in the first version). The controller review's `scheduleSpaceRelayout` already makes hiding and showing one layout, and what follows a closed window (who gets the keyboard, where the camera goes) looks at the layout, so putting that off underneath it broke scenario 21. An app that quits costs a layout per docked window.
- F30: arbor-sim's `fullscreen <id> on|off` plays the whole green button: the window covers its display and the other windows of that display (not of others: displays have Spaces of their own) are on a Space that does not show, then two seconds pass.
- F58: there are two strengths. `hold` is the spec's: nothing moves the view until `release`, not even the user's own keys. `hold still` is new: Arbor moves nothing by itself, and the user's keys, gestures, ⌘Tab and Dock clicks work. Some people are in calls all day; a view that cannot be moved at all is right while presenting one thing and useless the rest of the call. One verb, one way out (`release`), two lines in the menu.
- F58: macOS has no public way to ask whether the screen is being shared or recorded (`CGSessionCopyCurrentDictionary`, `SCShareableContent` and `NSWorkspace` do not say; the indicator in the menu bar belongs to Control Center and is not API). So holding is a mode, plus `holdStillFor`: a list of "bundle id, optionally part of a title"; while such a window is open, in whatever state and whether or not Arbor arranges its app, Arbor holds still. It never starts the strict `hold` by itself.
- F58: `holdStillFor` defaults to `["us.zoom.xos Zoom Meeting", "com.apple.FaceTime"]`. Both from memory, not seen on a Mac. Teams and Meet have no window that says "call" reliably, so they are not in it; the README has a recipe for Meet in Chrome.
- F58: `release` during a call that started holding still lasts until every matching window has closed. Releasing a `hold` during a call falls back to holding still, and says so.
- F58: holding still, a window out of view that takes the keyboard within 0.5 s of a click or of a modifier key going down or up was asked for (Dock, notification, ⌘Tab) and the view follows; anything else is the app's own doing. The window server keeps those times for everyone (`CGEventSource.secondsSinceLastEventType`): no tap, no permission. Key presses do not count, or typing would make everything "asked for"; so switching with Spotlight or a launcher is turned away. `hold` does not try to tell: it turns everything away. The 0.5 s is from `ax-events.txt` (reports within 0.1 s of a click) with room to spare; ⌘Tab has not been timed.
- F58: the keyboard goes back to the window in view that last had it, even one Arbor does not arrange (the call's own chat); failing that, as after any move of the view, to the one under the pointer or the top-left one.
- F58: one arrival is reported two or three times within 0.04 s on a real Mac; reports within 0.2 s count once. A window sent back three times in five seconds keeps the keyboard, still out of view: Arbor does not fight an app for long. The focus ring stays where it is while a window is on its way out, so that it does not blink.
- F58: while the view is held, arranging is refused for windows that are out of view, and whenever the result would bring anything into view that was not: floating or dragging away the only window showing, docking where the tree can only widen the view. A drop like that shows no preview; a docked window goes back to its place, a floating one stays where it was let go.
- F58: when a window closes and what remains of a held space is not a space of its own any more (the tree flattens a row in a row), the view narrows to the part with the keyboard rather than widen. One window goes out of view; nothing comes in.
- F58: a held window in macOS's full screen or on another macOS Space is away, not gone: the hold stays, and the display is not laid out until it is back. So on the other Space docked windows stay out of view while the hold lasts.
- F58: with several displays the hold ends when nothing is left of what it held on any of them. A window closing on the second display must not quietly end the protection of the one being shared.
- F58: `hold` with nothing in view on any display fails. `float-all here` ends a hold as `float-all` does, and both drop the rules that were waiting. `exclude`, `include` and `reload-settings` are refused while held: they can bring windows into view or resize everything. `save-view` works. Commands added later are refused until `Command.isAllowedWhileHeld` lists them.
- F58: while held, pinch and ⇧⌘-drag taps are removed rather than answered, so that no band is drawn on a shared screen. Shortcuts stay registered, so that a slip beeps instead of typing into the window being shown. No default shortcut, and URLs can neither hold nor release.
- F58: the menu bar icon is a lock (`lock.rectangle`) while held and the usual icon filled in while holding still. Commands the hold refuses are grey in the menu.
- F58: a camera or a microphone that any app has running is a call (CoreMediaIO and CoreAudio, "device is running somewhere": public, no permission, nothing is opened). Two switches, `holdStillWhileCameraIsOn` and `holdStillWhileMicrophoneIsOn`, both on; no "ask", because a question at the start of a call is what must not appear. The product owner agreed, on condition that an idle Mac with "Hey Siri" and dictation on reports no microphone in use; otherwise that switch ships false. Not tried on a Mac.
- F58: of audio devices only those with inputs and no outputs count as microphones. "Running" is said of a device, not a direction, so a headset that is one device would count while it plays music. Its microphone is missed; the camera or the call's window usually says the same.
- F58: the sensor listens for changes (`CMIOObjectAddPropertyListenerBlock`, `AudioObjectAddPropertyListenerBlock`, as Objective-See's OverSight does) and looks for itself every 30 s in case a listener was lost, all on its own queue, so that a daemon that is slow to answer cannot hold up the main thread. Off counts after five seconds, so that changing devices in a call does not end it. It does not run in sessions limited to test windows.
- F58: holding still, a new window that opens within 0.5 s of a click or a modifier key (⌘N) gets its dock rule at once, as ⌘Tab is followed. So a false alarm (a microphone some app never lets go of) costs little: only what apps do by themselves waits. A held view makes room for nobody.
- F58 (product owner): the reason for holding still is always findable: a remark in the menu and `status` ("holding still for 12 minutes while the microphone is on"; `holdStatus` is there for `doctor`). Starting and ending by itself shows nothing and makes no sound; only the icon changes. A refusal names the way out for someone at the Mac: "the view is held; release it from Arbor's menu, with <the shortcut, if one is bound> or with `release`". The two menu items carry a sentence each as a tooltip.
- F58 (product owner): whatever puts something of Arbor's in front of an audience asks the controller first. `mayShowUnasked` (false during either hold) for what Arbor would show or sound by itself; `mayShowPanels` (false on a held view) for what the person asked for: Go to…, the minimap on demand, the shortcuts, welcome and update windows. The three settings are "advanced" in F12's schema.
- F58 (follow-up): during either hold a new window that opens mostly off screen is not rescued to the middle of the display. It becomes a floating window out of view, remembers the middle as its place, and is kept out, even off a Desktop that is in view, until the hold ends (then it comes in, as rules that waited apply) or, holding still, until the person moves the view. Nothing is lost: it floats like any other, and switching to it brings it into view. Holding still, a window the person opened themselves is rescued at once.
- F58 (follow-up): F47's dialogs wait only on a held view (`hold`), and come in on release, pause, float-all and quit. Holding still they are centred at once as before: a call can last an hour, the person can go to that app meanwhile, and an app whose modal dialog cannot be found looks hung.
- F58 (product owner, on review of #61; replaces criteria 3 to 5 of the spec and the entries above on "away, not gone" and on several displays): **the end of a hold moves the view no more than the hold did.** It can come by itself (the time is up, the call is over) with the screen still shared. A rule that waited is applied only if a rehearsal leaves the view where it is and brings nothing into view, and not to a window the user has moved; otherwise the window goes on floating, and a release by hand says so ("released; Terminal was left floating").
- F58: "leaves the view where it is" is checked twice, on the node the view is on and on what the layout would show. They differ while what the view is on takes up no room (minimized, another macOS Space); either check alone was fooled (`HoldFuzzTests` found the first case, a mutation the second). "Moved" means that the window is no longer where its app put it (`appPlacedFrames`), so an app that moves its own window after opening it counts too: it floats on, which harms nobody. A rule that was not applied is dropped, not kept for the next hold.
- F58 (product owner): **a slip on a key is not a way out.** On a held view ⌃⌥⎋ releases the hold and nothing moves; the next press pauses. ⌃⌥⇧F is refused like the others. The menu and `arborctl` keep both. So the hold has a key without a new one being assigned, and every refusal names it.
- F58: that goes by command and source, not by chord: any shortcut bound to `pause` or `toggle-pause` releases, any bound to `float-all` or `float-all here` is refused. Only on a held view; holding still, the user's own keys work as ever and ⌃⌥⎋ pauses at once. During a call it falls back to holding still, as `release` does. `quit` has no default shortcut and was left alone. URLs cannot pause anyway.
- F58: the key does nothing more while it stays down. A slip can last, and whether macOS repeats a Carbon hot key that is kept down has not been seen on a Mac (believed not). If its release is never reported, the next press is taken for a repeat and the one after that pauses.
- F58 (product owner): **a held view never widens by itself.** Minimized and hidden count as away, as full screen does: no layout until the window is back or the hold is released. If it closed, the view stays held and empty, and the menu says "The view is held: nothing is in it". With several displays each stays held by itself, and the menu says so only when none shows anything of what it showed.
- F58: an empty view is a state, not a layout that was skipped: the display gets an empty `Layout` and Arbor draws nothing on it, so that no drop target or seam points at a node that has closed. No window is moved. On release such a display is laid out, and its view goes to what is around it: there it has to go somewhere, and somebody asked.
- F58 (product owner): `holdStillWhileMicrophoneIsOn` ships false and the camera's switch true, until it has been seen on a Mac that "Hey Siri" and dictation do not count and that muting does not end a call (docs/TESTING.md). `com.apple.FaceTime` is out of `holdStillFor`: without title text any window matches, minimized ones too, all day, and the camera covers a real call.
- F58: a signal that is switched off does not count for the five seconds' wait either. With the microphone on all through a call and not counted, switching the camera off ended holding still at once.
- F58 (product owner): words. `status` says "(12 minutes so far)" for since and "for 3 minutes more" or "until `release`" for until. `resize` and `dock` do not advise "step out first" on a held view. The menu has the state line and **Release** of docs/MENU.md, in the hold's own block until the menu model (F10) puts states first; the ticked row still releases too.
- F58, F12, F13: on a held view the settings commands that change something (`set`, `reset`, `bind`, `unbind`, `rule add` and the like) and `check-for-updates` are refused like `reload-settings`; the ones that only read, `version` and `launch-at-login` work. A settings.json saved meanwhile is read when the view is let go, however that happens; holding still it is read at once, because saving a file is the user's own doing. So this one thing can still change the picture when a timed hold ends: it is the user's edit, and never applying it would be worse.
- F58 (follow-up, after the product owner's review; replaces "until the hold ends" in the entry on windows that open off screen): what was kept out of view during a hold, windows that opened off screen and dialogs that opened there, comes in **when the view next moves**, not when the hold ends. The end can come by itself with the screen still shared, and ⌃⌥⎋ promises that nothing moves. Whoever goes to that app moves the view, so nothing stays lost; a pause, `float-all` and quitting bring everything in anyway. Going from `hold` to `hold still` lets nothing in either. The price: between the end of a held view and the next move, a new dialog that opens out of view waits too.
- F58 (follow-up): a rule that waited is not applied to a window that is out of view when the hold ends. It would leave the view where it is and put a window into it that nobody has seen (found by the fuzz test, as soon as windows that open off screen met the check on the end of a hold).
- F58 (follow-up; the product owner's safeguard): what waits must be findable meanwhile. A window that was kept out counts as out of view in `status` like any other; dialogs, which Arbor does not arrange, are counted beside them ("1 out of view and 2 dialogs"), also after the hold for as long as they wait. Going to an app (its Dock icon, ⌘Tab) brings in that app's own dialogs though the view does not move: an app blocked on a dialog gives the keyboard to the dialog, so no window's focus report follows. Other apps' dialogs go on waiting: ⌘Tab between two windows in view must not bring up the mail app's alert. Not on a held view; holding still only if the person did it.
- F58 (follow-up; found by the keeper of the Accessibility layer, agreed by the product owner): a dialog of **the app in use** never waits, not even on a held view. One window of Keynote is being shown, another is out of view, and Keynote puts an app-modal alert over that one: it takes no input, nothing is to be seen, and its Dock icon does nothing because it is in front already. The app in use is the app of the window in view that has the keyboard (where the keyboard is sent back to), not the app in front: one that pushes itself forward together with its alert is in front too, and that is the alert to keep from an audience. The window system is told before any dialog opens, so nothing depends on the order of events.
- F58 (follow-up), known and not solved: an app that came forward **by itself** with its alert during the hold, where nobody has clicked elsewhere since, is in front without being in use. After the hold a click on its Dock icon activates nothing, so nothing is reported and the dialog waits for the view to move. `status` counts it and ⌃⌥Space brings it in. On a held view the keyboard is with a dialog nobody can see until the person clicks the window they are showing. To be looked at on a Mac before more is built on guesses.
- F58 (the product owner's finding 8, met when F53 landed first): holding still, a window that is let through because the user switched to it becomes the window the keyboard goes back to at once. Its focus report comes before the view has followed it, so it was not noted, and the next app to push itself forward sent the keyboard elsewhere, which sends a summoned window away. `summon` is refused on a held view: it brings a window into view.
- F58, traces: that a camera or microphone went on or off is recorded and replayed as an event (`callSignalsChanged`, two booleans and nothing about which app), and so is the answer to "how long since the last click or modifier key" (a number of seconds, never what was clicked or which key).
- F58, #152: a held view with nothing left in it **should** look empty, so `freshLayout(of:)`, which tests and arbor-sim ask whether a layout was forgotten, says so, worked out afresh and not from what the hold remembers. No exemption: the check goes on working on held displays. The view in the model does not move meanwhile; it is the ordinary layout that falls back on the nearest space around it that has something in it.
- F58: of the commands that landed meanwhile, `go` and `summon` are refused on a held view (they bring something into view) and `name` with them until its owner lists it; `layout`, `spaces`, `version` and the like are queries; `trace on` and `trace off` are allowed, because nothing of a recording shows and that is how a hold that misbehaves on somebody's Mac is found out.
- F58, F09: live reflow (`reflowWhileDragging`, off by default) moves windows during a drag without a layout, so past the fence of a held view. A trial that would show anything from outside the fence is not shown; among the windows in view it works as ever. Found by the fuzz test once it looked after every move of a drag and not only at its end (282 of 8000 walks); the case tried by hand, dragging the framed window, leaked nothing.
- F58, F10: in the menu the state line and Release come first (docs/MENU.md, states 4 and 5) and the two rows head the Arbor section. On a held view Release shows the key that pauses, which releases first; Pause Arbor and Float All Windows stay ways out from the menu and show no key, because theirs do something else now. Hold This View is grey when nothing is in view. The menu bar item is a single lock while the view is held, instead of its buttons, every one of which would be refused; a click opens the menu. While the buttons show, nothing in the menu bar says that Arbor is holding still.
- F58, F10: "For a shared screen." stays in the tooltip though a display is never a screen in Arbor's words: what people do in a call is share their screen, as macOS and every call app say. The menu's test of words lets that phrase through and nothing else.
- F58 (round 2; the chaos lab's `parked-held` and two scripts of the presets' owner): "when the view next moves" is decided by the layout itself: it follows a move of the view (reason `.camera`), its view is not the last one's, and the view is not held. It had been approximated by "any command a held view would refuse", so `reset screenEdgeBand`, a `view 9` that goes nowhere and a `summon` of an app that is not open ended the waiting with no layout after it, and the window stayed in its corner with the Desktop in view. Per display: a move on one display lays out that one only, so the others' windows go on waiting.
- F58 (round 2): a window that opens mostly off screen is kept out of view only while **a window of its app is out of view**, as F47 asks of a dialog. That is why it opened there, and what makes it something from out of view. One that is merely larger than the display, or whose app remembers a display that is gone, would have opened over the view like any new window; kept out, it only went missing.
- F58 (round 2; the chaos lab's `focus-ring-held`): reports within a fifth of a second are one arrival, and nothing is done about the later ones. But an app can take the keyboard just *after* Arbor asked it back (a new window is activated once it is up), and then no further report says so: what is typed goes to a window nobody sees. So when the span is over Arbor looks where the keyboard is and asks it back again, within the same limit of three in five seconds. The line around the window in view is kept up while a request is under way, not for a length of time; once Arbor gives up it is around no window.
- F58, F23 (mended by the integrator at the landing; the second half decided by F23's owner): `"newWindows": "dock"` waits like any rule that docks, and docks when the hold ends. F23 settles a place in several rounds with the window itself, which cannot be rehearsed on a copy, but its plan takes no place after which the view shows anything but what it showed and the new window, and lets the window float otherwise. Taken on its owner's word and then tried: `HoldFuzzTests` has it switched on in a third of its walks, 8000 clean.
- F58 (round 2; the chaos lab): `float-all` is the panic button and fetches what a hold kept out, also on a display where nothing is docked, which it does not otherwise lay out. `float-all here` is about one display: what waits there comes in with its view, the rest goes on waiting. The rule behind both: the waiting never ends without a layout.
- F58, F49 (round 2; the chaos lab): pin wins over kept out. "Always in view" is the person's own word about that window, and a call pinned by a rule is what they want to see during a call, also on a held view, where it is a new window over the view like any other. The same goes for a summoned window.
- F58 (round 2; for the chaos lab's rule `held-view`): "the view stays where it is" is about what shows, not about a node. Docking a window that floats in view at the edge of the view makes a group of the two (F62) and the view is on that group: a new node, the same things to see. What must never happen is that a window that was out of view comes in.
- F58 (round 2): three ways in which a new window places itself were looked at and left alone: a place kept for it after a restart, a pending `layout … --open` and an awaited `summon`. In each the person asked for just that moments before; someone who starts a preset for a talk and then holds the view wants the late window in its place, not floating over the talk. The fence holds: a window that has just appeared is on screen anyway.
- F58 (round 2): a new node is no move of the view. Docking a window at the edge of the view makes a group of the two (F62), with the same things to see; a drop does that on a held view, and a rule that waited does it at the very end of a hold. Taken for a move, each let in what was kept out (74 and then 7 of 8000 walks of `HoldFuzzTests`). The controller tells the two apart already: a layout after a move of the view has the reason `.camera`, one after an arrangement `.structure`.
- F58 (round 2; found by the chaos lab's walks): **what is seen is what is held.** If the window the view is on was hidden, minimized or on another macOS Space already when the hold began, the nearest space around it that has something in it shows, and that is the view the person asks Arbor to hold. The fence was around the window that is away, so the next layout for whatever reason changed the picture, and `hold` could say "there is nothing in view to hold" with windows in plain view. The fence, and the question whether there is something to hold, go by what a layout shows.
- F58, F26 (round 2; found on the frozen trunk of release candidate 1): what is up already comes down when a hold begins. The minimap draws the whole arrangement, what is out of view with it, so it goes at either strength, also when a call begins by itself. On a held view the shortcuts' sheet, the heads-up line and the field for a space's name go too; "Go to…" is closed by any command. A call that begins takes nothing else away that the person has just asked for.
- F58, F10 (round 2): on a held view the menu greys out what would really be refused, not only what is off the list: Float Focused Window with one window in view only beeped. The rows that open a window over what is being shown (About Arbor, Show Log, Settings File…) are grey as well: they are no commands, so the list did not reach them. Quit Arbor stays.
- F58, F27 (round 2): holding still, a window asked for by name (Go to…: `find`, `reveal`, a row chosen) is let through: it is the person's own doing, though Return in a palette is neither a click nor a modifier key (the fix of F27's owner, in release candidate 1). The asking lasts two seconds and outlives the reports in between, because the window can be on another macOS Space: macOS slides over first and reports the keyboard most of a second later. Meanwhile whatever has the keyboard in passing is neither followed nor sent away: asked back to the window that had it, macOS would slide back to the Space just left. If it is still there when the asking has lapsed, it is dealt with then.
- F58 (round 2): a click on a tile of a held view is refused as the command it stands for (`zoom-node`), by the one path that refuses, so it is told why in the words a key is.
- F58 (round 2; the product owner, 2026-10-14, after a day played as a user): **out of the box Arbor holds only when asked.** `holdStillWhileCameraIsOn` ships false and `holdStillFor` empty, like the microphone's switch. A camera that is on is not a screen that is shared, and a sensor that has never run on a Mac must not start by itself, in every call, a state that turns the person's own launcher away. The same goes for a window title written from memory; it stays in the README and docs/SETTINGS.md as an example, marked so. Hold Still and Hold This View stay in the menu.
- F58 (round 2; the product owner): **whatever holding still turns away is said on screen**, once for each app and hold: "Terminal is out of view: holding still · ⌘Tab or ⌃⌥G goes there". It goes by time (half a second after a click or a modifier key), so a launcher, `open` in a terminal and a link whose browser is slow look like an app that pushes itself forward; in silence the second try ended the same and nothing said why. The keys named are the ones that are bound. Not with `messages` off, and not on a held view, where nothing of Arbor's appears but the reason why a key did nothing. Later, measured on a Mac first and without reading keys: what counts as asking grows. After release candidate 1: a hold belongs to the display it was made on.
- F58 (round 2; read, not run, by the minimap's owner, and true): a focus report nobody asked for unsays only what was said of its own window. macOS's answer to "who has the keyboard" can lag behind its report, and then the view follows a moment later; a report from the background in between (a pinned call that was raised) wiped the mark, and ⌘Tab was turned away. Found on the way, not F58's and passed on: without any hold, a background report of a window that is not pinned replaces the one look that is due, and the view does not follow ⌘Tab either.
- F58 (round 2): a settings command that a held view refuses is logged by its verb alone (`Command.textWhenRefused`). It logs itself when it runs, without what `rule add … title …` holds of a title; refused, it never got there and was written whole.
- (F57 follow-ups, 2026-10-14) Decided by the product owner: a new window is a guest of ANY view that does not show the Desktop, not only of a single window or tab group; a window found after a pause or at launch counts as new.
- (F57 follow-ups, 2026-10-14) When Arbor has to pick the focused window (the focused one went out of view, or nothing is focused), it picks the window in view that was used last; of windows never used, the one under the pointer (floating before docked), else the top-left one. Before, the pointer came first, which raised a framed window over its guest and depended on a dictionary's order.
- (F57 follow-ups, 2026-10-14) Decided by the product owner: when an app's last window is closed or minimized, macOS focuses nothing; a quarter of a second later Arbor focuses a window in view. The price: ⌘N right after ⌘W on an app's last window goes to the other app. To be watched on a Mac.
- (F57 follow-ups, 2026-10-14) `zoom` on a guest frames its host, not the Desktop; without an id, over its framed host, it leaves the framed view.
- (out of view follow-ups, 2026-10-14) A place out of view is judged by the display that would stop the window there: the one its top-left corner is on, else the nearest. Measured with one display only; the fake window system follows the same rule so that the two cannot disagree.
- (out of view follow-ups, 2026-10-14) Quitting or pausing with no display at all puts the windows that are out of view where the main display last was.
- F25 (decided by the product owner, 2026-10-14, in place of "only the first matching entry counts"): for each key the first matching entry of `displays` that sets it counts; first match wins, as for rules, but key by key. The first rule made the spec's own example fall flat: a Studio Display is 2560 points wide, so `>=2500` took it and its own entry never applied. `displays` says which entry gave each value.
- F25 (product owner): a display's number (`2`, `@2`) is refused as a `match`, in words: numbers change with the arrangement of the displays. `set gap 50` and `get gap` say which displays keep a value of their own.
- F25: `match` compares the width in points only (`>=`, `>`, `<=`, `<`); a name matches if it contains the text, ignoring case ("Studio Display (2)" matches "Studio Display"); a UUID must match whole.
- F25: per-display values are bounded like F12's schema bounds the general ones (gap and screenEdgeBand 0 to 100, minimum usable sizes 0 to 1000); an entry outside them is skipped with a reason, not clamped.
- F25: options follow what a display is now (`main`, its size), not which display it was when Arbor started.
- F25: `displays` is a query like `rules`: text for people, answered before Arbor has started, refused from URLs. F50's `displays` (pointer, focus, JSON) can extend it.
- F25 (bug bash 1): a display's name is never logged, as the product owner ruled for events. Replies that quote one ("…; Studio Display keeps 20") are scrubbed on their way to the log as the names of spaces are, F12's own line about a change goes without the note, and a skipped entry of `displays` is logged by its number (its `match` may be a name).
- F48, F40 (the product owner's decision): a pause does not touch what is in view, so that someone framed on a virtual machine cannot see it. Only windows out of view are moved: back on screen, not raised, at the frame they floated at if F48 remembers one, otherwise in their slot with everything shown. Neither "as on quit" (two reshuffles, to frames that may be days old, and the window the person paused for shrinks) nor "show everything" (the same shrink). **Replaced the same day by Q21.**
- F48, F40 (the product owner's decision): quitting during a pause starts the window system once more, puts windows back where they floated, and stops, with no layout in between. (B6:) only windows that are still where the pause left them; one that somebody has moved meanwhile is theirs.
- F48: excluding an app puts its docked windows back where they floated (a floating one that was out of view goes back to where it floated, not to the middle of the display).
- F48: `float-all` and quitting also give minimized and hidden windows, and windows on other macOS Spaces, their frames back; a pause does so for those that were out of view.
- F48: a window dropped to float stays exactly where it was dropped, even if the Desktop moves away from under it as its old slot closes; from then on it is a floating window the user put there, like any other.
- F48: a window with nothing remembered keeps its slot's size when floated.
- F48: after `float-all` the layout does not tidy windows onto the Desktop (a gap smaller than the display all round), so a window that touched or hung over a screen edge is back exactly there.
- F48: a window in macOS's full screen is not moved by `float-all`, a quit or a pause; it goes back to its slot's frame when it leaves full screen with Arbor gone. macOS does not let it be moved.
- F48: floating the framed window moves the view to the closest one that shows the Desktop, or onto the Desktop itself if it would be a tile there; the move is not recorded for Back, because the space it left no longer exists.
- F48: "as it was" is exact, also for a window that hung over a screen edge: people leave windows there on purpose. A remembered frame is fitted into its display only if it is no longer a place for a window: larger than the display, less than half on it, or with its title bar under the menu bar. The same now goes for floating windows that were out of view at a quit.
- F48: letting go also undoes what docked windows did to windows that floated all along: `float-all`, quitting and excluding put a floating window that was pushed aside or squeezed onto the Desktop back where the user left it.
- F48: a frame remembered on one display and used on another keeps its distance from the display's top-left corner.
- F48: what is saved wins over what is found at launch: Arbor cannot tell a window the user moved while it was not running from one a crash left in its slot.
- F48 (the product owner's review of #41): a window floated by command is put there on purpose, like a dropped one. If the Desktop in view is smaller than it (the narrow one `arrange` makes), it keeps its own size, on the Desktop as far as it fits and over the docked windows for the rest, kept on the display; it is not squeezed. The two ways to float agree.
- F48 (the same review): a window that grows at the drop slides back onto the display as far as the pointer stays on its title bar (sideways all the way; upwards until the pointer is 28 points below its top).
- F48, F57 (the same review): a window that starts to float while the Desktop is out of view (`float`, `toggle-float`, a drop) is a guest of the view it floats over, so Back brings it back. With `newWindowsWhileFramed: "float"` it is not, as for new windows. (B6:) toggling a guest twice makes it a guest of the same host again where docking it did not bring the Desktop into view; where it did (its host sits beside the Desktop at the top level), it ends up on the Desktop, in view.
- F48, F40 (the same review): `pause` replies "…; windows that were out of view are back on screen"; `float` on the framed window replies "w101 is floating; showing the Desktop".
- K027: a floating window counts as on the Desktop by the part of it that shows; what hangs over the edge of the screen is in no Desktop and says nothing.
- K027: a window its user left hanging over the left, right or bottom edge of the screen goes on doing so where the Desktop reaches that edge: it is not pulled in by a layout, by the Desktop coming back into view, or by the two gaps of slack. Not if less than half of it would show (a frame left over from a larger display), and not where its app opened it.
- F60, 2026-10-14: whether an icon or widget is under a held press is asked while the button is down, from a queue of its own, never from the tap's callback. A click nobody answered for by the time the button comes up is given back: a sweep can be undone with another click, a click an icon never got cannot.
- F60, 2026-10-14 (the product owner's design): the zone is the whole view of a display with something docked, and the way out is the REAL frame of any window Arbor knows to be in view, floating ones included; so the wallpaper around a docked window that cannot fill its slot is guarded. Observers now hear of moves without a layout (`.frames`), which keeps the tap's copy fresh.
- F60 / K071, 2026-10-14: one rule for "bare Desktop". ⇧-drag asks the same three questions as the plain click. It differs in two things: it may start on a tile or a gap handle (Q11), and only a drag known to start on the wallpaper is Arbor's. The pinch keeps the window list alone: a pinch over an icon taken by Arbor harms nobody.
- F60, 2026-10-14: gap handles are left out of the guard: a press there begins a resize and lands on Arbor's panel, not on the wallpaper.
- F60, 2026-10-14: "something docked" counts windows docked on the display even when the view shows only the Desktop: a sweep would take the floating windows.
- F60, 2026-10-14: the README and the guide keep the advice to change the macOS switch until the guard has been seen at work on a real Mac.
- (controller, small things, 2026-10-14) The framing shortcut remembers for five seconds the window it un-framed into a tile, so that it goes back and forth; a move of the view or the user's own switch to a third window ends that.
- (controller, small things, 2026-10-14) A floating window that cannot be made as small as the Desktop sits in the Desktop's middle and overlaps the docked windows on both sides alike (product owner's note on #17), within the screen.
- (controller, small things, 2026-10-14) A window unpinned over a view that does not show the Desktop becomes a guest of that view.
- F51 (product owner, 08:49 UTC): the first way home must not spring back for a slow press.
