A hold in which nothing was chosen (no pointer move beyond the slop, no arrow key, wherever they led) and that lasted under 1 s counts as a tap: everything stays, one history entry. From 1 s on, letting go without choosing returns. ⎋ returns at any time.
- F51 (product owner, 09:01 UTC): after landing by peek ⌃⌥↩ goes to everything, not one level out: the peek stood in for Show Everything, and ⌃⌥[ is there for the view it began from. Only while the landing is the last move in history.
- F51: what closes while the keys are down reaches Back, Forward and the view that was left as at any other time. (Copies of the two lists, put back when the key came up, missed that a group had given way to its last member; and with the view on everything, whatever came to mean everything was tidied out of history.) The workspace knows that the view is only visiting and keeps its books by where it belongs. With nothing left of the view that was left, letting go stays on everything, as before.
- F51: what is written to `state.json` with the keys of a peek down is the stay the look would become: everything shows, and Back leads to where its user was. (The workspace was written as it stood, without a word on the visit. History is kept by where the view belongs, so with Back leading to everything, where the camera is meanwhile, what was read back had tidied the entry away, and after a crash or a quit in mid-peek Back had forgotten.) Ended once, where it is written, and not by every reader: no new key in the file, and nothing for the next way of starting to forget.
- K126, 2026-10-14: the sentence takes no side on who gets the key ("one of the two does nothing"). The backlog's fix assumed that macOS wins and asked for the label to leave the menu; the one measurement there is (macOS 26, two input sources) says Arbor wins. So the label stays, and `check-F44.sh` records who wins wherever it runs.
- K126, 2026-10-14: `CopySymbolicHotKeys` says which keys macOS uses and whether they are ticked, not what for. Names come from a table of where macOS puts its shortcuts by default (22 of them, from memory of System Settings); a shortcut somebody moved is "a shortcut of macOS's". The ids in `com.apple.symbolichotkeys` would name those too, but that file lists only what was changed.
- K126, 2026-10-14: the two input-source shortcuts are ticked on every Mac, so they count only with two or more input sources; an unknown number counts as one (F42: unknown is never a problem). A shortcut with the Globe key is left out, except on arrow and function keys, whose events carry that bit anyway.
- K126, 2026-10-14: the clashes are about the shortcuts in effect, not the ones held at this moment: a pause or an app that keeps its keys does not hide them. With `hotkeysEnabled` false there are none.
- F10, F60: while `guardDesktopClicks` is on, "Click wallpaper to reveal desktop" is no problem to `doctor` (a note under a ✔) and no point in the welcome window's Desktop & Dock row; with the guard off it is both, as before. The README still recommends "Only in Stage Manager" until the guard has been seen at work on more Macs.
- F42: `doctor` prints problems first, then notes, then what is in order; within a kind, the order the checks are made in.
- F42: apps known to use ⌃⌥ themselves (F41's list) are a note and a "Good to know" row, never a problem: Arbor only ever suggests Shortcuts Off.
- F42: the keyboard layout is a ✔ line that names the layout and the shortcuts that stay at their US position; collisions on the layout are among settings.json's skipped entries, as `bindings` reports them.
- F42: every ✘ of `doctor` is written to the log once at launch, after shortcuts and event taps have been tried ("setup: …").
- F10, after the product owner's letter: the heading is "Focused Window", the login tick "Open at Login" in the menu and in the welcome window (macOS's own words).
- F46: "the first launch" is the first start with no `welcomed: true` in `state.json`, so whoever used Arbor before the welcome window existed sees it once too. Any showing counts: at a launch without the permission, from the menu, by `welcome` or `check-setup`, by opening Arbor again. `ARBOR_NO_PROMPT` neither opens it nor counts. A state file set aside (unreadable, or after a rescue) means one more welcome.
- F46: at that launch the window opens on its first page if a row is required or "best changed", otherwise on the three moves. Screen Recording and what is only good to know hold nobody up.
- F46: for the three moves the window goes to the right of the display, 24 points from the edge, once per showing; after that it stays where it is put. It does not watch where windows dock.
- F46: whether macOS is hiding the menu bar item is not detected; the footnote says what to do if it is.
- F10 (product owner's note on #73): when macOS leaves the row of buttons out of a full menu bar Arbor draws its single icon instead. It remembers the narrowest width that was left out, tries anything narrower, and tries everything again after a change of settings or displays and when the displays wake. No setting: `menuBarControls` false is the way to have the icon always.
- F10: macOS's answer to "is the item showing" (`occlusionState` of the button's window) is believed only after it has once been yes on this Mac, and is not asked where the menu bar hides itself. Nobody has seen what it answers behind a notch, or on macOS 26, where Control Center hosts the items. An answer that is always no, or always yes, leaves everything as it was.
- F10: `doctor`'s "Menu bar item": ✔ showing; – showing as a single icon (no room for N points); ✘ not showing, if it has been seen before; – "not showing, as far as macOS says", if it never has; – unknown here.
- F10 (same note): with nothing docked only Show Everything is ticked, in the menu and among the buttons, though the Desktop is the same place.
- F10 (product owner, for release candidate 1): the welcome window and `doctor` do not speak of Screen Recording for now (`Controller.offersScreenRecording`, false). With that permission Arbor takes pictures of windows for its motion (F02), and the one time it did so on a Mac it crashed; the mended code has not run since. The row and its tests stay, for the day a setting (`windowPictures`) switches both on.
- F10: that day has a switch now. `offersScreenRecording` is the `windowPictures` setting (#184) and nothing else: whoever turns the pictures on is told in the welcome window and by `doctor` which permission they need, and nobody else hears of it.
- F10 (product owner, 13:27 UTC, through triage): row 13 is "Keep This Window in View", with no app's name: it pins a window; the app is what a rule is for. One row, greyed out when no window is focused.
- F10, F63: the welcome window counts the permission as there only if macOS's flag and Arbor both say so. Arbor's probe can be ahead of the flag, and the window that opens because the permission went must not show a tick.
- F10: state 8 of `docs/MENU.md` ("one line, as in `status`") shows `Controller.notices` (#127) up to the first bracket, with the whole notice as the tooltip. The longest is 145 characters with its file names, which would make the menu as wide as a 13-inch display.
- F02: a camera move is a zoom of the whole plane, not "shrink toward the parent": windows out of view are where the map that carries the camera's space onto the screen puts them (x and y scaled independently). The documenter's reading of TrellisWM's simulation has since confirmed it (T43).
- F02: the plane is laid out at the two views' common ancestor, not always at the root as the spec's "projected frames" say. Gaps do not scale, so a root layout seen through a zoom differs by a few points from what is on screen; with the common ancestor everything in view starts exactly where it is. From or to Show Everything the two are the same.
- F02: a window that leaves the view is slid on until it is off the display altogether. The plane puts it just beyond the view, which can still be behind the Dock or the menu bar, where it would hang until the motion ends and then vanish.
- F02: a window out of view at both ends is drawn if the motion carries it across the display (from the first of three columns to the third, the second passes by).
- F02: windows in a tile travel as the tile draws them (app colour and icon, at their place in the tile) and turn into themselves on the way. Out of view, a window looks as it does at the end where it can be seen.
- F02: nothing fades in place except what has nowhere to go: a tab that becomes active during a camera move, a floating window growing out of the Desktop's tile, a window that was minimized while out of view.
- F02: one full-display stage per display with a layer per window, instead of a panel per window. It needs a backdrop, because the real windows jump underneath: a picture of everything below the windows (wallpaper, icons, widgets) with Screen Recording permission, otherwise the wallpaper's file, otherwise dark grey; never clear.
- F02: the stage draws every window Arbor can see on that display, also the ones it does not arrange and the ones that stay put, stacked as the window server stacks them. Windows it cannot see (dialogs, sheets, panels, picture-in-picture) are covered for the length of the motion.
- F02: the stage sits one level above floating windows, below the Dock and the menu bar, and lets clicks through.
- F02: a second command retargets the motion from where things are (the swarm's shared design decision) instead of finishing it (the spec). A mouse button going down anywhere finishes it (T42); a key press does not.
- F02: the curve is a cubic Bézier (0.2, 0, 0, 1): an ease-out, somewhat stronger than Core Animation's own, without overshoot. `Transition.curve`, for anything else that moves.
- F02: animated: camera moves, commands that change the arrangement, drops, releasing a window's edge, windows closing, minimizing, returning, docked by a rule. At once: gap drags, putting a moved window back, macOS Space switches, display changes, settings (also `exclude` and `include`), launch, pause, resume, quit, anything while the user holds a window, and whatever moves nothing by more than a point (so switching tabs is instant).
- F02: `animationDuration` is kept between 0 and 2 s; a value that is not a number counts as 0.46.
- F02: pictures are kept between motions instead of being discarded after each (the spec), because a motion cannot wait for one: taken at launch, 0.4 s after the focus moves, at the start of a motion and 0.3 s after it. In memory only, let go of when the window leaves the screen and when Arbor pauses or quits. At most 3 million pixels each (12 MB), so small windows are as sharp as the display and a full-screen one on a Retina display is a little softer (the brief said 1x; soft text the moment the stage goes up would give the trick away).
- F02: if an app does not do as it is told (refuses its slot, rounds its size), the controller sends a corrected transition right after moving the windows.
- F02: `float-all` leaves bringing back windows that are out of view to the relayout, so that they fly in instead of appearing first. They end where they did before. Since F48, which decides where they go, `float` and `float-all` hand their moves to `relayout(…, puttingBack:)`: no real window moves before the stage is up.
- F02: `ARBOR_NO_PICTURES=1` runs Arbor as it is without Screen Recording permission, for looking at that on a Mac that has it.
- F02 (coordinator and product owner, for release candidate 1): pictures of windows ship off, `windowPictures: false`, an advanced setting. The only time they were taken on a Mac Arbor crashed in the first sweep, and the fix has never run; with the welcome window offering the permission, that would be a crash at the first zoom, three restarts by the watchdog, and Arbor down. Colours and icons are the path that was filmed. While it is off no picture is taken or kept, whatever macOS allows. It goes on by default once somebody has seen them work.
- F02: the click that ends a motion also reaches the controller (`WindowSystemEvents.pointerPressed` → `Controller.pointerPressed` → `finishAnimations`), so that tests reach T42. The director keeps its own monitors: they see the other buttons and clicks on Arbor's own panels, and two ways down are better than one.
- F02: another tab of a native macOS tab group coming to the front is applied at once. Its app has just shown the switch, there is no picture of the new tab, and Terminal's 31 points for a tab bar are not worth a stage.
- F02: of Arbor's own windows, the ones that never take the keyboard (`canBecomeKey` is false: tiles, gap handles, the ring, heads-up panels) are not windows to `stackedWindows()`; the settings and welcome windows are.
- (F13, 2026-10-14) The version reads `0.9.0 (build 214, 47a2ba9)`: the `VERSION` file, the number of commits, the short commit, with `-dirty` for uncommitted changes to what goes into the app. `status` keeps its one-line message for scripts; `arborctl status` prints the build on a second line.
- (F13, 2026-10-14) Automatic update checks (`automaticUpdateChecks`, off; the name is the product owner's) run a minute after starting and weekly by the clock, not by one long delay, which would stand still while the Mac sleeps; a check that got no answer is tried again after six hours; a check by hand puts the next automatic one off by a week. Nothing is saved, so every launch starts with a check a minute later.
- (F13, 2026-10-14) An automatic check never opens a window (product owner: the user may be in a call); what it found changes the menu item to "Update Available…" and shows in `version`. A pause holds the automatic checks. A check by hand always shows its answer, "No update information" included. A check that got no answer does not take back a release seen earlier.
- (F13, 2026-10-14) The answer is shown in an ordinary floating window, not an alert: an alert's modal loop would keep Arbor from hearing about other apps' windows while it stayed open.
- (F13, 2026-10-14) The release page's address is made from the release's tag, and only after the tag has parsed as a version; the address GitHub sends along is ignored. A tag that is not a version is "no update information".
- (F13, 2026-10-14) `launch-at-login [on|off]` is a command (T73); `version`, `check-for-updates` and `launch-at-login` are refused from `arbor://` URLs. The binary takes `--version` and `--launch-at-login [on|off]` from a shell without becoming the app, which is how `make uninstall` removes the login item when Arbor is not running.
- (F13, 2026-10-14) The local signing key cannot be exported, and only the key with our label has its partition list changed, never the keychain's other keys. No trust setting is made: neither codesign nor the permission database needs one.
- (F13, 2026-10-14) An identity other than ad hoc and "Arbor Local Signing" is taken to be Apple-issued and gets the hardened runtime; the secure timestamp, which needs the network, only in `make release`.
- (F13, 2026-10-14) `make install` links `arborctl` into `~/.local/bin` if that is on the `PATH`, else `/usr/local/bin` if writable, else `~/.local/bin` with a hint. It never uses sudo and never touches Homebrew's folders.
- (F13, 2026-10-14) A release's tag is `v<VERSION>`, or `v<VERSION>-<suffix>` for a pre-release, which update checks do not offer; the workflow refuses any other. Releases are created as drafts; a draft left by an earlier run is replaced, a published release is never touched. The job that publishes runs on Linux and is the only one with a token that can write.
- (F13, 2026-10-14) The release workflow starts by hand only. A pushed tag (release candidates are tags) and a pull request start nothing: the pull request that carries the trunk into `main` contains the release tooling, so every push to the trunk would have started a universal build on a Mac.
- (F13, 2026-10-14) For scripts (product owner): `version` is one line until something has been asked about updates, then a second line says what was found; `launch-at-login` without a word answers `on` or `off` (`off: …` with the reason when it is switched off in System Settings), while `on` and `off` are answered in a sentence. `status` keeps the build on a second line: it is what people paste into a bug report.
- (F13, 2026-10-14) The recommended way to let codesign use the new key is macOS's own dialog (Return at the script's question, then Always Allow); typing the keychain's password into the script is the convenience, and the script says what that costs (product owner).
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (menu): `docs/MENU.md` decides the order, grouping and titles of the whole menu. The top level is full; a state line is followed by the command that ends the state; a command has one title everywhere.
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (scripts): one name per idea in events (F24), queries (F50) and `state`: `docs/GLOSSARY.md`, "Names scripts see". `docked` true or false; `state` in-view … other-space, `minimized` with a z; `label` for Arbor's words, `name` only for a name a person gave; `display` the number, `displayID` the UUID; exit codes 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 64; `arborctl`'s own options anywhere on the line.
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (F58, against criterion 5 of the spec): while the view is held ⌃⌥⎋ releases the hold and nothing moves, the next press pauses; ⌃⌥⇧F from the keyboard is refused. A held view never widens by itself: a framed window that is minimized or hidden counts as away, one that closed leaves the view held and empty. When a hold ends, a rule that waited is applied only if it leaves the view where it is and brings nothing into view. `holdStillWhileMicrophoneIsOn` ships false until seen on a Mac.
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (F61, F29, F23): an empty Desktop may give way down to a tile, but what Arbor took it gives back while a window floats on it: not when the window opens, at the next layout that a move of the view or a change of the arrangement causes. One function for the three features.
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (F40): a resume ends with the focused window in view. (F18, pending slots:) after a resume no place is kept for a window closed during the pause. A crash while paused comes back paused, if the watchdog restarted Arbor.
- Product owner, 2026-10-14, on #59 (F08): a change of displays ends with the focused window in view, as a resume does and by the same means. Executor: only when a display came or went, because the Dock grows at every minimize and such a report must move no view; and the view moves before anything is laid out, so that every window moves once. The keyboard is left where it is.
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (F51): a hold of ⌃⌥Space under 1 s during which nothing was chosen counts as a tap: everything stays.
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (F53): a summoned window that was sent away is out of view in every view, Show Everything included; `home` (⌃⌥0) makes stashed and summoned windows ordinary, so after it nothing is out of view, without exception.
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (F57 follow-up, to be tried by a person): after the last window of an app closes or is minimized, the window in view that was used last gets the keyboard.
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (F62): the gap at the end of a kept group that runs the same way as its row resizes only the last window and the neighbour, as in a flat row.
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (privacy): names a person gives (spaces, presets) are not logged, like window titles. Log numbers.
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (F16, F55): ⌃⌥ + scroll zooms; ⌥ alone pans, and is claimed only while `continuousZoom` is on, something is docked and the pan does something.
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (F60, reversing the verdict on #69): `guardDesktopClicks` ships false until session 3 of `input-taps.sh` and R1's list (an open menu, Spotlight, Control Centre, a Dock stack, Arbor's own menu over the Desktop, Mission Control, tap to click) have been seen on a Mac. A sweep is undone by one more click; a click that vanishes looks like a broken Mac.
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (F16, F55, replacing the line above on ⌥): ⌥ + scroll alone is never Arbor's. The view slides during a pinch of Arbor's and with ⌃⌥ + two fingers sideways; ⌃⌥ + scroll up and down zooms; the dominant axis at the start decides.
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (K160): while the display is mirrored Arbor holds still (`holdStillWhileMirroring`, true).
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (K153, #123): a window that leaves before anything else in its row has changed gives its room back to the one it took it from; otherwise T06.
- Product owner, 2026-10-14 (F51): after landing by peek, ⌃⌥↩ goes to everything.
- Core follow-ups (K061): an arrangement nests 100 levels deep at most, which is as deep as `state.json` is read back. A dock that would make it 101 is refused and changes nothing; the command says why, a drop shows no preview and ends as a drop on nothing does (a docked window back in its slot, any other floating where it was let go). T04 says "as deep as you like"; nobody gets near 100 by hand.
- Core follow-ups (K061): between displays a window leaves its own arrangement only once it is known that the other will take it.
- Core follow-ups (K153): a window that leaves before anything else in its row has changed gives its room back to the one it took it from, so a window that opens beside another and closes leaves it as wide as it was. After any other change to the row (an arrival, a departure, a resize, a swap), and when it is the other of the two that leaves, T06 holds: the whole row shares. A fuller version (whoever leaves, in any order) was built and dropped, because it contradicts T06 and its test.
- F12: ranges, chosen so that no value in range can hide every window or divide by nothing: `gap` 0 to 100 pt, `minimumUsableWidth` and `minimumUsableHeight` 0 to 1000 pt (0: never a tile), `screenEdgeBand` 0 to 100 pt, `windowEdgeFraction` 0 to 0.5 (at 0.5 a window has no middle left), `arrangeDesktopWeight` 0.1 to 3, `pinchThreshold` 0.05 to 2. `arrangeDesktopWeight` and `pinchThreshold` are "advanced".
- F12: values are checked in one place, when settings.json is decoded (`SettingsSchema.sanitize`) and when `set` runs. In the file, a number out of range moves to the nearest end of the range; a value of the wrong type, a number that is not finite, an unknown choice or text that means nothing goes back to the default. Each costs its own key only, is logged once per load and is listed by `settings`. The file itself is not corrected.
- F12: `set` of the value a setting already has, `reset` of a default, `bind` of what the shortcut already runs and `unbind` of a free shortcut succeed, say so, and neither apply nor save anything.
- F12: while settings.json is not a JSON object, Arbor keeps the settings in use and **refuses `set`, `reset`, `bind`, `unbind` and `rule …`** rather than save over a half-finished edit; each first looks whether the file has been mended. `reset everything` is the way out: it replaces the file. The same holds for a file that is broken at launch, when Arbor runs on the defaults.
- F12: a settings.json that disappears while Arbor runs is left alone (editors can leave such a moment): the settings in use stay, and the next save writes a new file. `reload-settings` without a file still starts from the defaults and writes them, as a launch does.
- F12: `reset all` keeps `bindings`, `rules` and `excludedBundleIDs`; `reset everything` does not; `reset <key>` works on those three too.
- F12: `unbind` silences a shortcut altogether (null for one that has a default). Binding a shortcut to its default command removes the entry, which is how a default comes back. `bind` replaces without asking and says what the shortcut ran; it refuses what `bindings` in the file may not hold (queries, keys that would stop typing).
- F12: `rule add <bundle id> <action> [title <text…>]`: the title comes last and takes the rest of the line. Rules are numbered from 1 as `rules` lists them, `rule move <n> <place>` gives the place the rule ends up at, and `rule <bundle id> <action>` without `add` still works (it was arbor-sim's own command, now gone).
- F12: in command text, whichever of space and slash comes first separates the whole text, so the other can be part of a word (`bind ctrl+opt+/ desktop`, a title with a slash). `dock/left` and `dock left` work as before; a mixture (`dock 101/left`) no longer does. `slash` is another name for the `/` key.
- F12: the settings commands work before Accessibility is granted, unlike `exclude` and `include`.
- F12: the title pattern of a rule is never logged, so `rule add` logs the rule without it and `bind` logs the shortcut without its command.
- F27: `find` (⌃⌥G) while the palette is open closes it, as the key that summons Spotlight does. With no windows at all it fails instead of opening an empty palette.
- F27: the palette's list is a snapshot from when it opened; nothing is added, removed or reordered under the user's hands. A choice that has gone meanwhile beeps, says so, and refreshes the list with the text kept.
- F27: order with nothing typed: windows that had the keyboard, latest first, the focused one after them; then the rest in layout order; then saved views by display and number. Named spaces would follow.
- F27: ranking is match quality, then less text left over (counting only the texts that matched, so three Safari windows tie for `saf`), then the list's order. Points nobody observed anywhere: letter 16, word start +8 (twice for the word's first letter), capital or digit boundary inside a word +6, following the previous match +10, in the name +4, a gap -3 and -1 for each further letter.
- F27: searched and shown: 80 characters of a name, 200 of a title (line breaks shown as spaces); 8 words and 64 letters of a query. The rest is ignored.
- F27: beyond case and accents, ß = ss, æ = ae, œ = oe, ø = o, ł = l, đ and ð = d, þ = th, ħ = h, ŧ = t, ı = i, ς = σ; curly quotes and all dashes equal the typewriter's; kana voicing marks, Hebrew points, emoji skin tones and invisible characters are ignored; an unfinished Korean syllable finds the finished one.
- F27: a window on another macOS Space or in full screen is only focused (macOS switches); the view follows when its Space shows, so the Space being left is not rearranged. `reveal` fails while a window is being dragged.
- F27: the pointer is never moved. The palette opens on the display F56 says commands act on, and after it has taken the keyboard to another display, shortcuts act there by that same rule. F27's own rule for this (`displayGoneTo`) was taken out when F56 landed.
- F27: going to a saved view on another display leaves the keyboard where it is, as `view <n>` does.
- F27: `arbor://` URLs may run neither `find` nor `reveal` (product owner): the palette takes the keyboard, and a link that fetches a window by words in its title, un-minimised and focused, perhaps on a shared screen, is more than moving the view. `allowAllURLCommands` allows them.
- F27: `find` is logged without its text, which is likely to be part of a title; the reply that quotes it goes to the caller only.
- F27: no `windows` query of its own (product owner): it is F50's command, with the one vocabulary scripts see. F27 had one until just before it landed.
- F27: `reveal` and the palette go to a window in full screen although other commands refuse one (F30): they only look, and focusing it is how macOS shows its Space.
- F27: the focus of a window still taken to be on another macOS Space is reported after the rescan that finds it here, not when its app comes forward (agreed with the Accessibility layer's owner).
- F27: windows Arbor was told to leave alone (Don't Arrange, `ignore` rules) are listed after the others as "not arranged" (product owner). Choosing one brings it back from the Dock or ⌘H and focuses it where it is. They come by app and number: Arbor keeps no record of when they had the keyboard. An Arbor limited to some processes (`ARBOR_ONLY_PIDS`) lists nobody else's.
- F27: named spaces are listed after the windows and before the saved views, all of them, also a name two displays share. The name of a space typed in full outranks every other match (product owner), so `go-to Writing` and `go Writing` agree.
- F27: a stashed window is "out of view until summoned" and is summoned by being chosen, through the focus report, as by ⌘Tab (agreed with F53's owner).
- F27: no text panel of its own for names: F20's `NamePanel` is the one-field panel, for presets too.
- F27: displays are called what macOS calls them (`NSScreen.localizedName`), "display 2" without a name, "DELL U2720Q (1)" and "(2)" for two of a make; with one display nothing is said.
- F27: `reveal` moves the view at once, as if a window on its way back from the Dock or from ⌘H were back already, instead of waiting for macOS's reports: it must show the window even if the window does not get the keyboard.
- F27: the palette always closes, and the controller sees to it as well as the panel: a window other than the one it opened over reports the keyboard, a drag begins, the displays change, anything is gone to (also by `find <text>`), `pause`, quit. It does not open during a drag. Other commands (a shortcut that moves the view) leave it open; its notes on where windows are may then be out of date.
- F27: the palette is 600 points wide, a quarter of the way down, nine rows of 38 points, corner radius 16 (the brief said 14; macOS 26's windows have about 16), `.popover` material. A Mac has compiled it and shown it on screen (arbor-e2e); nobody has looked at a picture of it yet.
- (the level is kept, 2026-10-14) Decided by the product owner: when the view is on one window or tab group and the user goes to a window out of view (⌘Tab, the Dock, Go to…), the view frames that window instead of stepping out to the smallest group that holds both. A floating target: the Desktop fills the view; a guest: its host. From a group or everything nothing changes. One step of history. Added by the executor: a view on the Desktop alone counts as a view on one space too, or the frame would be lost on the way back from a floating window.
- F10 on the trunk of 09:45 UTC: `BuildInfo` is the one version format (`doctor`'s Version, About Arbor; `SystemFacts.version` and `MacFacts.version` are gone), and there is no version line in the menu (the product owner: About shows it). Check for Updates… is in Help ▸ above About Arbor, under the controller's title. Open at Login goes by `loginItem.status`: ticked when enabled, unticked when disabled or switched off in System Settings (choosing it leads there), absent when unavailable.
- F10: `docs/MENU.md`'s state 7: "Settings.json has a mistake: line 3, column 3" (where, not what) with Settings File… under it; at most two states show. `doctor`'s settings.json row quotes `settingsFileProblem`, `valueProblems`, `skippedRules` and the bindings' problems; F10's own check of `frameRectangleModifier` is gone.
- F10: in the schema `menuBarControls` and `historyButtons` are normal and `menuBarFill` advanced, until there is a hidden level, which is where the product owner wants it.
- (S3 traces, 2026-10-14) `trace` commands are closed to `arbor://` URLs: a page must not be able to start a recording. Everything else about traces is in Q14.
- (S3 traces, 2026-10-14) A trace lists the app name and bundle id of every window that was open, not only of those that moved; `make trace-check` shows them before anything is committed.
- F53: a summoned window that is sent away is *stashed*: out of view in every view, Show Everything and the Desktop included, until summoned again. Otherwise the second press of the key does nothing wherever the Desktop is in view. It is counted in `status` ("1 out of view"); ⌘Tab or its Dock icon summons it; docking, pinning or `float-all` (even with nothing docked) makes it an ordinary window again; pause and quit bring it into view.
- F53: it comes to the display under the pointer, like camera commands (Q12 and F56 may change both together). Frames are remembered per display and per app (bundle id, or name without one); the first time on a display it is centred at its own size; a remembered frame is fitted into the display.
- F53: `<app>` is a bundle id or the app's name, in any case; names may have spaces. Letters, digits, space and `.-_&+'()` only, 255 bytes, no leading dot.
- F53: "most recently used window": by Arbor's focus history among the app's windows that can show; never-focused windows by lowest number.
- F53: what ends a summon: the command again; a focus report for a window of another app that really has the keyboard; `summon` of another app on that display, or of a docked or pinned window; docking, pinning, closing; `float-all`; pause and quit (which move nothing). Not: another window of the same app, background focus reports, a click on the wallpaper (nothing reports a window).
- F53: the keyboard goes back to the window that had it only when the command sends the window away and the window still has the keyboard. If that window is gone or out of view, a window in view gets it (never a pinned one).
- F53: with `summonStays` the window is treated like a pin while it stays: through camera moves, raised when covered. Summoning another app still replaces it.
- F53: a minimized window or a hidden app is asked for through `openApplication`, like an app without a window, and summoned when it shows, within 5 s. A window that comes later is an ordinary new window. A rule that docks the new window wins.
- F53: pausing and quitting turn "summoned" into "stashed" without moving anything. Killed while summoned, it is still summoned at the next launch.
- F53: a display is unplugged: stashed windows stay out of view on the main display and return stashed; the summoned window comes into view on the main display and is not pulled back while it is summoned.
- F53: `arrange` leaves summoned and stashed windows out; dragging a summoned window never docks it (as for pins).
- F53: `summon` is refused from `arbor://` URLs by default, because it can launch any app on the Mac and any web page can open a URL. `allowAllURLCommands` allows it.
- F53: no default shortcut; ⌃⌥` and ⌃⌥' are reserved for it in `docs/KEYBOARD.md`. Words: "summon" in `docs/GLOSSARY.md`; "stash" stays in the code and in `state`.
- F53, product owner's verdict (2026-10-14): the stash is accepted, with one change: `home` (⌃⌥0) empties it (stashed windows are ordinary floating windows on the Desktop again), because "a window is gone: ⌃⌥0" may have no exception; `show-all` (⌃⌥Space) leaves it alone; the summoned window becomes an ordinary window too, because the keyboard goes along with the view, which would send it into the stash the next moment. The reply to the second press says how to get the window back. Go to…, the minimap and F43's count of windows out of view must list or count stashed windows like any other (`state` has `"stashed"` and `"parked"` on them).
- F53: summoned and stashed follow macOS's own window tabs (⌘T in a summoned Terminal is still the summoned window, F17); summoning a window that opened over a framed one (a guest, F57) ends the visit. The first summon puts the window on whole points.
- F53: the product owner's notes on #40 hold for a summoned window as well: a command that moves the view takes the keyboard to the window that arrives (which sends the summoned window away, unless `summonStays`); `zoom` in it un-frames what is beneath, and `zoom <id>` fails with "w103 is summoned; dock it to give it a place"; docking it says "and it no longer comes and goes with `summon`".
- F53, after F56 landed: `summon` goes through `cameraDisplay()`, so it now comes to the display that has the keyboard, or to the pointer's if the pointer moved last, like every command that names no display. Sent away on a display where nothing else is in view, the keyboard goes to a window in view on another display rather than stay in the window that is out of view.
- F53, where it met the rule that a resume ends with the focused window in view: pausing still ends the summon (the window is stashed in what is saved, and not touched). At `resume`, if the keyboard is still in it, it is summoned again where it is, as a sent-away window is whenever it gets the keyboard; if the keyboard went elsewhere during the pause, it goes out of view.
- (F09 T10, 2026-10-14) `dropDwell` is 0.15 s, kept between 0 and 2. Windows, tiles and the middle of a window (tabs) wait; screen edges, the Desktop and the window's own slot do not. "The same target" means the same node and edge on the same display.
- (F09 T10, 2026-10-14) a window let go before its target has been held long enough is not dropped there: a floating one stays where it is, a **docked one goes back to its slot** (the product owner's decision; at first it was undocked where it lay, but word of a drag reaches Arbor about 0.3 s late on a Mac, so every brisk drag ended that way). What the preview has not shown does not happen.
- (F09, 2026-10-14) the key that turns docking off during a drag is **⌘, not the spec's ⌥** (the coordinator's decision): since macOS 15, ⌥ held during a window drag brings up macOS's own tiling preview. It counts on any move, when the hold ends and at release, with whatever else is held, and it undocks a docked window even over its own slot. There is no setting for the key.
- (F09 follow-up, 2026-10-14, from the independent review) ⌃⌥⌘ held during a drag does not turn docking off: it is the beginning of Arbor's own shortcuts (⌃⌥⌘ + arrow). ⌘ with anything less still does.
- Product owner, 2026-10-15 (IDEAS I21, "a window would not dock on the right while another window was minimized"): **a key acts on what a person sees: one press, one step that shows; a step that changes nothing to the eye is no step.** `arrange` of three makes a column of two beside the third. With one of the two in the Dock (or hidden) the other fills the column, and the key that moves it took it out of a group nobody could see: the same picture, both windows narrower, "moved" for an answer. Decided: **a group that does not show trades places as one.** A pure swap, every size as it was, each window moves once; the group, its name and its saved views stay whole; restored, the other window comes back to the one it shared the column with, on whichever side that is by now (the price: not where it last was on screen). What is away is no neighbour to pass either: where nothing that shows lies beyond, the answer is "already at the … edge", said on screen. Where the group shows nothing changes: the first press takes the window out of it.
- (F09, 2026-10-14) anything that lays a display out during a drag (a command, a window opening or closing, a display change) hides the preview and the target has to be held again. No layout moves the window being dragged.
- (F09 T09, 2026-10-14) live reflow shows what letting go at that moment would produce, except while a target waits to be held: then it shows the arrangement without the window, so the neighbours close up as soon as the pointer leaves the slot and do not open it again for every target passed. The old slot still means "back where it was", the pointer is otherwise matched against the arrangement without the window, and the gap the others open counts as the target that opened it.
- (F09 T09, 2026-10-14) a trial layout places floating windows by the same rule as a real one (`placeFloatingWindows`), so they also go out of view and come back with the Desktop; when the drag ends each is put back as it was unless the drop itself moves it.
- (F09 T08, 2026-10-14) `keepPushed` matters only with `reflowWhileDragging`. Either way, when the drag ends pushed windows go where a drag without reflow would have left them, so neither setting changes where anything ends up.
- (F09 T11, 2026-10-14) the preview fades in and out and glides between targets in 0.12 s, not at all with "Reduce motion", and independently of `animationDuration`. `previewStyle` is not implemented: there is one style.
- (F09, 2026-10-14) a drag cut short (its release is lost, another drag begins, the window closes or becomes a native tab, Arbor pauses or quits) puts a docked window back in its slot and leaves a floating one where it is. There is ONE watch for a lost release, F17's in the Accessibility layer (`dragCancelled`): F09's own, which dropped the window where the pointer was, is gone, because where the pointer is by then says nothing.
- (F09, 2026-10-14, the product owner's decisions on #49) only the Desktop (also as a tile) or ⌘ takes a docked window out of the arrangement: let go over the middle of another window or in a gap, held or not, it goes back to its slot; a floating window stays where it is let go. With live reflow the old slot does not open again over such a place. With `previewDrags` off nothing has to be held: nothing would show when the hold is over.
- (F09 T09, 2026-10-14) a floating window that a trial layout brought into view with the Desktop goes out of view again when a later trial takes the Desktop away, although a real change of the arrangement never takes a floating window out of view (#54): it was out of view before the drag, and nothing has been decided.
- K1152: a `state.json` that is read with repairs, or with a display left out, is copied as it was to `state.before-repair.json` (then `.2`, `.3`; the first is never written over, the last holds the latest) before Arbor saves over it, and `status` names the copy until the next launch. Any repair counts, also a harmless one from an older version's file: telling them apart is one more thing to get wrong, and a copy costs nothing.
- F54: a name in `layout` is the app's name or bundle id in any case; failing that the last part of a bundle id (`Chrome`), failing that whole words of the name (`Code`). Only the best kind of match counts, judged over all windows. `WindowSlot.assign` and `AppName.rank` are the one place that decides, for presets (I2) and F50's windows-by-app too.
- F54: windows in view are preferred to minimised, hidden and other-macOS-Space ones, then most recently focused first, then oldest first. A window that is away can still get a place and takes no room until it is back; the reply says so.
- F54: windows come over from other displays when a line names them (focus order does not stop at a display). Only the target display's other windows float.
- F54: every floating window of the display is moved onto the Desktop after `layout`, not only those it undocked: the docked space is all new, so none of them is over it on purpose.
- F54: a line that finds no window at all fails and changes nothing (a typo should not float everything). `layout Desktop` floats everything and succeeds even when nothing was docked.
- F54: the Desktop that is added is `arrangeDesktopWeight` times the **average** size beside it, so that `A:2 | B:2` and `A | B` are the same arrangement.
- F54: sizes go from 0.001 to 1000 in thousandths. Printed, the smallest window or group of each group has no size (the Desktop never sets the unit), which makes printing stable: print, parse, print gives the same line.
- F54: `layout` shows everything; `back` returns to where the view was. Saved views and history follow the spaces that still hold the same windows (and the Desktop or not); the rest are dropped. (Product owner's review of #48.)
- F54: a Desktop the line does not mention is widened to `minimumUsableWidth` while windows float on it, unless that would make a tile of a docked window. `layout` lays out as a change of the arrangement, so it never takes a floating window out of view; those that were out of view and stay so are counted in the reply.
- F54: among the windows a slot could take, the one already in its place wins (after windows named by number and titles that are found), so a line applied twice never swaps two windows of an app.
- F54: a whole line in quotes is unwrapped when it holds `|`, `/`, `(` or `[`. From a URL, a line that names no window is refused like `float-all`.
- F54: an app whose windows Arbor does not arrange is reported as such, not as having none, and `--open` does not open it. An app whose only window another slot took is "only 1 window of", not "no window of".
- F54: until F04's strips, the reply counts the windows behind tabs and names ⌘Tab and ⌘`.
- F54: displays are counted `@1` the main display, then left to right, then top to bottom; an id from `state` works too. F50's `displays` should print these numbers.
- F54: beyond the spec, a number is a window (`412`, `w412`; `layout --ids`) and `App~text` prefers a title (`layout --titles`). A title is a preference, not a filter: if no window has it, the slot takes another window of the app. With `*` it is a filter.
- F54: parts of titles in a line are written `~…` in the log (`Command.logText`), as rules leave their title patterns out.
- F54: which tab shows is not written; it is the group's most recently focused window.
- F54: `--open` opens at most 12 apps, in the background (`open -g`), waits 60 s, and comes before rules but not before `ignore`. The wait ends as soon as the display's tree is not the one `layout` built. A latecomer never moves the view. It is refused from URLs.
- F54: plain names are letters, digits, `.`, `-`, `_`; all other punctuation needs quotes, which keeps every other sign free for later. `,` in particular stays free for F50's several commands in one call.
- (F23, 2026-10-14) One mechanism for new windows: `"newWindows": "dock"` is short for a last rule `{"app": "*", "action": "dock auto"}`. So rules win by coming first, late titles, F57 and F58 treat it like any rule, and `rules` shows it. Default `"float"`; which default TrellisWM's behaviour calls for is still Q13.
- (F23, 2026-10-14) `*` at the end of a rule's `app` stands for the rest of the bundle id (`com.jetbrains.*`); `*` alone is any app, also a window without a bundle id.
- (F23, 2026-10-14) The guards against small (under 400 x 300 as opened) and fixed-size windows belong to `dock auto` and to every `*` rule that docks, not to rules that name an app in full: whoever writes `com.apple.calculator` and `dock right` means it. A window that is passed over goes on to the next rule.
- (F23, 2026-10-14) "The focused window" is the docked window showing in the view of that display that had the keyboard last, because a new window has the keyboard before Arbor hears of it. For one second a window docked automatically counts as that window, so a burst spirals inward whether or not focus reports keep up.
- (F23, 2026-10-14) Beyond the brief: when neither half of the focused window's slot has room, and after a tab if tabs are on, the largest window in view shares instead. On a 1440 x 900 display that is eight docked windows instead of four before new ones float.
- (F23, 2026-10-14) "Room" means that everything that was showing still shows, the Desktop included, and nothing else comes into view. Automatic docking never makes a tile and never moves the view; it floats the window where its app put it instead.
- (F23, 2026-10-14) The new window is resized to its slot before anybody makes room, so one that refuses disturbs nobody; a docking that squeezed a neighbour into a tile is taken back at once. Sizes learned on the way are kept.
- (F23, 2026-10-14) With the view on the Desktop alone (and windows docked elsewhere) a new window floats: that is where floating windows live. Framed on one window it is F57's guest.
- (F23, 2026-10-14) Nothing docks by itself while the mouse button is down: a tab torn off a browser is a new window in the user's hand, and the drop decides.
- (F23, 2026-10-14) New seam event `windowFound`: a window first listed after a macOS Space switch was open all along, so it is adopted like a window found at launch (it floats; of the rules only `ignore` applies). This also ends F11's "windows first seen after a Space switch count as new".
- (F23, 2026-10-14, after the product owner's note on #105) The first window docked automatically, with nothing docked and nothing floating in sight, leaves the Desktop what Arrange Windows gives it (`arrangeDesktopWeight`, a third of the display); with a window on it the Desktop keeps half, as after `dock right`. An empty Desktop that keeps giving way, and gets back what Arbor took, is left for one rule with F61 and F29.
- (release manager, 2026-10-14) A release candidate is built once the way `make install` builds it before anybody installs it: `scripts/candidate-check.sh`, run on a Mac for the one commit that is to be released. Not for every commit: it takes minutes of a Mac's time. `ARBOR_CONFIGURATION=debug make install` is the way out if only the optimized build fails.
- F05, 2026-10-14 (product owner): `frame-rect` that changes nothing to look at fails with "that is already the view", also with nothing docked; it used to answer "framed everything". What the view shows counts, not which space it is on: a group whose other windows are minimized looks like the one that is left.
- F48 (found by the real-window suite): the frame remembered for a floating window grows, on the side its app refuses, to the size the app took when Arbor pushed it onto the Desktop. A size the app no longer allows is nothing to go back to, and it made the window hang off the right edge of the screen. It never shrinks that way.
- F08, F49 (found by rehearsing `check-F49.sh`): while the displays macOS has are not the ones Arbor was last told of, a window that moves was moved by macOS, not by the user. The report of a display coming or going waits until the displays have settled, and macOS moves windows at once; before, a floating or pinned window got a new home that way and did not go back with its display, and docked windows were sent to where no display was. A move the user makes in that second and a half, other than by dragging, is not remembered. Whether apps report those moves through Accessibility is for a Mac to show (`check-F49.sh`, the display part).
- F08 (found by rehearsing `check-F52.sh` against a fake second display): after a display that only blinked there is no report, so nothing put back the windows macOS had cleared off it. The controller now does, two seconds after the last such move, once the displays are the ones it knows; while they are not it asks again. What macOS has put back itself by then is not touched, so this costs nothing if macOS always does, which nobody has recorded.
- F08, the limits of that: it knows only of moves that apps report; whether they report what the window server does to their windows when a display goes has not been measured. A window the person moves in those seconds by other means than a drag counts as moved by macOS, and is now also put back. Arbor asks for half a minute after the last move whether the displays are the ones it knows; after that it was no blink. And a window out of view that macOS brings onto another display meanwhile is not sent back to its corner before the displays are known: the corner may be that of a display that is gone.
- F08, K1811 (bug bash of 2026-10-14; agreed with the coordinator, R5 and R1): the frames a workspace remembers are global, so they move with their display's corner: for a connected display in `updateDisplays`, for one that was away when it returns (`DormantWorkspace.origin`), and at a launch or a resume (`PersistedState.origins`). Both keys are optional. A file without them (written before this) is not trusted: a floating window that is in view on its display is remembered where it is, any other frame is fitted onto the display; that costs a window left hanging over an edge on purpose its overhang, once. The three rules for what is a place for a floating window (`reach`, `putBack`, `showsOnlyASliver`) are not touched: R5 has the net.
- F19 x K1811 (coordinator, TRIAGE, 2026-10-14): an undo step holds workspaces and frames in the coordinates of the displays as they stood. When a display that STAYS gets another FRAME, corner or size (the lid, System Settings, a change of scaling; a corner also during a pause), the undo and redo history ends on every display: ⌃⌥Z has nothing to undo. Blunt on purpose, for rc1. The size came second (coordinator, 14:44 UTC, on the product owner's yes of 14:18: "losing fifty steps is nothing beside losing a window"): H2 ran dock, another scaling, undo: 120 of 700 points showed and `home` did not fetch it. F19's `floatingWindowsDoNotGoBackToAPlaceThatIsNoLongerOnTheDisplay` expected that undo to work and now expects it to refuse: a change of decision, not of the test's purpose; both windows must still end on the smaller display. A display that goes with the others' corners unchanged leaves their history alone, as F19 designed and tests. Shifting the steps instead (`workspace`, `changedTo` and `frames` of each) is the better cure and is H13's to judge after rc1.
- F49 follow-up: Arbor also looks whether a pinned window is covered when the left button goes down anywhere (`WindowSystemEvents.pointerPressed`, from the Accessibility layer's existing monitor of the mouse), every 0.2 s while it stays down, and three more times after. A click in the window that already has the keyboard brings it to the front and reports no change of focus: every click in the editor one is working in buried the call. Found by rehearsing `check-F49.sh` on the fake Mac. With nothing pinned a click costs one check of a flag.
- (F23 with F61, 2026-10-14) Automatic docking plans with the room F61 will make: a placement counts if everything still shows once gaps have moved. On F61's terms an empty Desktop may become a tile for it; a Desktop with a window on it stays usable, and the newcomer floats instead.
- F43: the **menu** is answered on screen like a shortcut or a gesture (the spec said never). Newcomers are the ones who use the menu, and a menu item that beeps explains nothing. `arborctl` prints its own reply; URLs stay silent, because any web page can open one and must not be able to put words on the screen.
- F43: what earns a line at the default level, `messages: "helpful"`: every failure; the success of a command whose effect cannot be seen (`save-view`, `pause`, `resume`, `toggle-pause`, `exclude`, `include`, `reload-settings`, `float-all`, and `undo` and `redo` once they exist); the three hints. Nothing else: what a key did is on screen already, and a line for every key press is what makes people switch such lines off.
- F43: with `messages: "off"` a shortcut or menu item that fails beeps, as before; otherwise the line replaces the beep. Gestures never beeped and still do not. Text in `messages` that is none of the three levels counts as `helpful` and is logged.
- F43: each hint is shown **three** times ever (the spec), not once: a line that fades after a few seconds is easily missed. A hint counts only when it was shown, so `off` and scripts use none up.
- F43: hints are read from what changed on a display, not from which command ran. So a window dropped on an edge, a clicked tile, a rule that docks a new window and ⌘Tab teach too. What a script changes teaches nobody, and neither does what Arbor restores at launch, after a pause or when a display comes back.
- F43: the out-of-view hint comes only when everything was in view before. Walking from one framed window to the next would otherwise use it up in three key presses. Its words are the glossary's; with one window out of view it says "window is".
- F43: "Docked · ⌃⌥↩ frames it" comes when exactly one more window is docked than before; after Arrange Windows there is no "it".
- F43: when several things could be said, one is: a failure, else the out-of-view hint, else the tile hint, else the dock hint, else the reply.
- F43: the line stays 1.5 s, or 0.06 s a character if that is longer (about 17 characters a second), at most 6 s. The longest hint stays 4.4 s. Not a setting.
- F43: the line's bottom edge is 56 points above the bottom of the display's visible frame, centred, at most 720 points wide (less on a narrow display), then it wraps. With the minimap showing it is to sit 10 points above that (`HeadsUpModel.placed(in:above:)`).
- F43: sentences name the keys in effect. Where a command has none, another that does the job stands in (⌃⌥⎋ for `resume`, ⌃⌥0 for Show Everything, ⌃⌥F for `float`); failing that a hint drops the part that names keys, and a reply that quotes a command uses its menu name in quotation marks (“Save View 3” saves where you are looking). With `hotkeysEnabled` false no keys are named.
- F43: advice in a reply that only makes sense at a terminal (a clause or a bracket quoting a query or a usage line, such as "`state` lists the windows") is left out on screen. Replies go on being written for the terminal.
- F43: `show-shortcuts` toggles. The panel lists what `KeyBindings.effective` returns, one row per shortcut, except that ⌃⌥1–9, ⌃⌥⇧1–9 and a full set of four arrows are one row each while complete under one set of modifiers. It fails, with the reason, when shortcuts are switched off or all removed, and while paused. Not accepted from URLs.
- F43: the panel ends with a part the spec does not have, "Pointer and trackpad" (as `docs/KEYBOARD.md` does): drag to a screen edge, to a window's edge, a gap, click a tile, ⌥ + pinch, ⇧⌘ + drag, ⇧ + drag on the Desktop; the last three follow `gesturesEnabled`, `frameRectangleModifier` and `frameRectangleOnDesktop`. Dragging to an edge is the first thing a newcomer has to learn, and this is where they look. A feature that adds a gesture adds a row to `CheatSheetModel.pointerRows(settings:)`.
- F43: any command except a query puts the panel away, from whatever source, because Arbor's own shortcuts never reach the panel and "any key" has to include them. The command still runs.
- F43: the panel takes the keyboard (without activating Arbor) so that the key that ends it is not typed into the document behind. It leaves without fading, because a fading key window would swallow what is typed next. Auto-repeat of a key held since before it appeared does not end it.
- F43: a pause removes the line and the panel like every overlay and then says "Paused until ⌃⌥⎋; every window is back in view" for 3 s: the one thing Arbor shows while paused. A pause asked for by a script shows nothing.
- F43: `Command.title` falls back to the text form in title case ("tab next" → "Tab Next"), and `Command.group` to "Arbor itself", so commands added on other branches need no entry to appear in the panel; being listed in the help puts them in the right group.
- F43 (product owner's review of #37): a success that left the views, the arrangement, every window's frame and the keyboard focus as they were is said at the level `helpful`, as an unseen change: to the person who pressed the key it looks like a dead key. So ⌃⌥0 with everything in view says "Showing everything".
- F43 (same): two outcomes get a line of their own instead of the reply. ⌃⌥↩ on a floating window whose Desktop is in view already: "Mail floats · ⌃⌥⌘← docks it, then ⌃⌥↩ frames it" (without the hint if either shortcut is gone). Any command on the focused window of an excluded app: "Arbor does not arrange Mail · Arrange Again in the menu".
- F43 (same): on screen a bundle id becomes the app's name where Arbor has met the app; a first word with a dot in it (settings.json) keeps its small letter; an alternative that a dropped terminal note explains goes with it ("click one, or name one (`state`…)" is "click one"); " on the display under the pointer" and " on the focused window's display" are dropped, because the line is on that display.
- F43 (same): when an app that keeps its own ⌃⌥ comes to the front within a second of something a person asked Arbor for, the line says "Shortcuts are off in Emacs · ⌘Tab leaves, ⌃⌥⎋ pauses". Not after switching there by hand, not after a script, and not after `pass-through`, which says its own piece.
- F43 (same): in such an app `show-shortcuts` lists every shortcut in effect, under the heading "Off in Emacs, except ⌃⌥⎋" in place of "Keyboard Shortcuts"; sentences there name only the shortcuts still held.
- F43 (same): the answer to a command given during a pause (`resume`) goes to the display chosen after it ran, so "Resumed" shows where "Paused" did. The menu says "Show the Desktop", as the panel does. "(⌃⌥?)" is out of the documents until the key that types ? is what ⌃⌥⇧/ means on every layout (#97).
- F43 (after F20, F53 and F54 landed): `name`, `unname` and `launch-at-login on|off` count as unseen changes. `ask-name`, `check-setup` and `welcome` are their own answer when they succeed, at every level, like `show-shortcuts`. A name typed into the field is not confirmed by a line: the field runs its command as a script does, and its closing is the answer.
- F43 (same): a window that `summon` sent away does not count as out of view for the teaching moment: the person sent it there, and ⌃⌥Space, which the hint names, does not bring it back. At the level `helpful` sending it away is silent, like every change that can be seen.
- F43 (same): titles: "Name This Space" (`ask-name`), "Name This Space “Work”", "Remove This Space’s Name", "Remove the Name “Work”", "Go to “Work”", "Summon Notes", "Arrange as Safari | Mail" and "Open and Arrange as …" (without the parts of window titles a line can hold), "Check for Updates", "Open at Login", "Don’t Open at Login". A command with a default shortcut must have a title somebody chose (`Command.listedTitle`); a test says so to whoever adds the shortcut.
- F43 (same): the debug line `said on …` follows the log's rules, not the screen's: names of spaces are ‹name›, what a reply quotes from settings.json is left out, and the reply to `set`, `bind`, `rule` and their like is not repeated at all.
- F43 (same): the line is announced to VoiceOver at medium priority, as two sentences ("Docked. ⌃⌥↩ frames it"). The panel of shortcuts is not: it takes the keyboard.
- F43 (bug bash 1): one panel has the keyboard at a time. ⌃⌥⇧/ closes the name field before the panel of shortcuts takes the keyboard; ⌃⌥N puts the panel away, as any command does.
- F43 (same): the end of a peek is no teaching moment, whether the view goes back or lands somewhere: whoever held ⌃⌥Space knows where the windows went. The facts are brought up to date all the same.
- F43 (same): any change of displays (one unplugged, the last one gone, another shape or place) puts the line and the panel of shortcuts away: their places were worked out for the displays as they were. Also during a pause; and a line the pause left on a display that went meanwhile goes when Arbor resumes.
- F43 (same): a title in the panel of shortcuts is cut at 48 characters. `bindings` shows the whole command.
- F43 (same): on screen a space's name is said as it was written, also one that reads like Arbor's shorthand (w12, (n5), a command in backticks).
- F43 (same): the same words at another place (the line making room for the minimap) are not announced to VoiceOver again; the same words at the same place (the key pressed again) are.
- F43 (same, with F27): `find` without words is its own answer, opening and closing: the palette is there, or gone. With words it goes somewhere and is answered like any command that moves the view.
- F43 (same, with F58): a line that is up when a hold begins, by command or because a call started, goes at once, unless it says why a key did nothing. A key that a held view refuses is answered "The view is held · ⌃⌥⎋ releases it" (the keys in effect for `toggle-hold`, `release` or `toggle-pause`; without any, "Release it from Arbor’s menu"). `release` and `toggle-hold` count as unseen changes, so "Released" is said; that a hold began is not, since nothing may be said then. With `messages` off there is no beep during a hold.
- F43 (bug bash 1, from triage): a success whose reply says that something else went with it ("…; 2 saved views are gone", "…; 1 name is gone", "… and it is no longer kept in view") is said at the level `helpful`, comes before any hint that is due (the hint keeps its showing), and names the way back: "Arranged 4 windows; 2 saved views are gone · ⌃⌥Z takes it back". A loss is read from the reply's words (`HeadsUpText.namesALoss`), the only place where commands say it; tests run the commands that do.
- F43 (same): the Desktop with nothing on it, turned tile, is not "the first tile". What automatic docking tries and takes back teaches nothing: the layouts on the way are not looked at, only what came of it (`summingUpForTheLine`), so one new window spends one hint at most.
- F43 (same): the hint about tiles names a key as well: "Too small to show: click the tile to zoom in · ⌃⌥G goes there too".
- F43 (same, the product owner at 13:27): what holding still turns away is said, once per app and hold: "Terminal is out of view: holding still · ⌘Tab or ⌃⌥G goes there". It counts as a problem, which is what may be said during a hold. Not on a held view, where the audience is, and not with `messages` off. The hold's owner wrote the same at the same time (`holdSaysThatItTurnedAway`, Controller+Hold.swift) and landed first: theirs is the one that stands, F43 adds two tests.
- F43 (same): `find <words>` that finds nothing is "the reply to `find`" in the debug log: the words may be part of a window title.
- F43 (same): after a peek nothing is said. Triage's sentence ("Held: a peek. Tap ⌃⌥Space, or ⌃⌥0, to stay.") would be a fourth teaching moment and is the product owner's to want. That a timed hold or pause has ended is not said yet.
- F43 (the product owner on #277, 13:42): in the panel of shortcuts ⌃⌥G reads "Go to…", with its dots, as in the menu; a reply that ends in the held message ends on screen in the same few words ("Safari fills the view, and the view is held · ⌃⌥⎋ releases it"); a window Arbor does not know is called by its number after the word window ("There is no window 999").
- F15: `dimBehindArranged` defaults to 0.25 (the spec said 0; TrellisWM ships `dimDocked` on at 0.3), so that the docked area reads as one surface out of the box; `blurBehindArranged` defaults to 0.
- F15: the surface is every slot in view (leaf rectangles and tiles, the Desktop's tile included) grown by half the gap, rounded up to a whole point, and merged; it stops half a gap short of the screen edges and never covers the Desktop's rectangle.
- F15: `blurBehindArranged` is an amount from 0 to 1, not points: macOS gives apps one strength of blur, and less than 1 fades it.
- F15: one key `gapHandles`: `shown`, `hidden` (draggable, nothing drawn), `off` (the spec's `showSeamHandles` and F33's `gapHandles` in one; the product owner's decision).
- F15: `tileStyle`: `icon` (default, the icon alone as in TrellisWM), `name` (icon and app name), `plain` (colour only). The Desktop's tile follows; a group's tile always shows its miniature and a count.
- F15: a tile is the neutral grey of light or dark mode with `appColourAmount` of the app's colour mixed in, so tiles are pale in light mode and deep in dark mode (TrellisWM's are always a darkened app colour). Text on it is black or white, whichever contrasts more.
- F15 meets F20: a name somebody gave a space is drawn on its tile whatever `tileStyle` says, `plain` included: it is their own word, and the only one that tells two tiles of one app apart. A window's and the Desktop's tile caption it; a group's capsule holds the name in place of the number of windows (the miniature shows how many), and the number where there is no room for the beginning of a name.
- F15, seen on a Mac (the off-screen test with Apple's own icons, 2026-10-14): on a fill of middling lightness the better of black and white ink only just reaches 4.5 to 1, and not on the half of the fill shaded towards it. Such a fill moves away from its ink, towards white under black and black under white, until the ink has 5 to 1 on all of it. Pale and deep fills, which is all of them at the default `appColourAmount`, are untouched.
- F15: an app's colour is the most prominent vivid hue of its icon, not the average, which is near white for a glyph on a white tile and mud for a many-coloured icon.
- F15: the Desktop's tile is smoked glass over the real wallpaper, with a white glyph, in both modes, and casts no shadow; other tiles are opaque and cast a window's shadow.
- F15: the handle in a gap is white with a soft shadow (accent while dragged), not a label colour, because it lies on the wallpaper.
- F15: tiles, the drop preview, the backdrop and the focus ring share one window corner radius: 17 on macOS 26, 10 before; the backdrop's outer corners are that plus its reach.
- F15: values out of range are clamped, values that are not numbers and unknown choices fall back to the default, each with a line in the log; `settings.json` keeps what was written.
- F15: no command and no shortcut switches the backdrop; ⌃⌥B stays reserved and unbound (`docs/KEYBOARD.md`).
- F15: pictures taken on a person's Mac for the repository hold the script's own windows only (`scenery capture`, `scenery curtain`); whole-screen pictures (`ARBOR_SHOTS=1`) are never pushed.
- (controller, round 3, 2026-10-14) Decided by the product owner: floating windows are out of view only when the Desktop is not PART of the view. A Desktop too small to use, a tile, is part of it: its windows are in view at their own frames, fitted into the display, over the docked windows and in front of them. Nothing is pushed there. Added by the executor: a window Arbor itself laid over such a view makes way again once the Desktop is usable; a window that opens over such a view is no guest.
- (controller, round 3, 2026-10-14) A floating window that no display shows is out of view whoever put it there, also one the user had placed over docked windows: the next layout with the Desktop in view puts it on the Desktop.
- F18 (T21): `soleFloatTakesDesktop` is **off** by default, unlike TrellisWM's. Only the key and its default are known; a floating window that changes size by itself surprises whoever did not ask for it.
- F18: nothing is stretched where no docked window takes up room (nothing docked, all of it minimized, a macOS Space of its own), so that Float All Windows still means a normal Mac.
- F18: company is a floating window that takes up room on the same display. Minimized, hidden, full-screen and excluded windows, and windows on another macOS Space, are none; nor are pinned windows (F49). Guests (F57) sit on the Desktop while it is in view, so they count.
- F18: a window its user moved, resized or dropped while it was alone keeps that frame until it has had company or been docked. Being away itself (minimized, hidden, another macOS Space) does not end that. It is not saved: after a relaunch Arbor fills again.
- F18: a title-bar drag of a window that fills the Desktop, ending on the Desktop, gives it its own size back under the pointer, as macOS's own tiling and F48 do, rather than leaving it the Desktop's size a few points further on.
- F18: any window dropped on the Desktop by hand stays where it was dropped, also a docked one dragged out onto an empty Desktop; `float` and new windows take the Desktop.
- F18: a new window that is the only floating one takes the Desktop wherever its app opened it, although other new windows are left where they open.
- F18: at launch and on resume floating windows are left where they are found, as before; the window takes the Desktop at the next change to the arrangement or the view.
- F18: pinning the window that fills the Desktop gives it its own size first, and that is where it is pinned. Unpinned while alone, it takes the Desktop at once, like a new window, although F49 lets an unpinned window stay put otherwise.
- F18: a pause leaves the window that fills the Desktop as it is, like everything in view (F40 since F48); quitting, also during the pause, gives it its own frame back.
- F18, overruled by the product owner (note 1 on #85): at launch the window that floats alone takes the Desktop once, two seconds after the last window has turned up, instead of waiting for the next change.
- F18 (note 2): `float` on a window that floats alone and was taken in hand makes it take the Desktop again. `toggle-float` (⌃⌥F) still docks a floating window.
- F18 (note 3): with no button down, a window that fills the Desktop, stays in its corner and only shrinks by up to 36 points (the largest text cell, as in F45's size steps) is its app settling, not the user.
- F18: the setting is listed as advanced.
- App shell, product owner's notes on #18 (2026-10-14), taken: (1) a pause outlives a crash: `state.json` records the pause and its end, and only an Arbor the watchdog started follows it; a person who starts Arbor gets it running. A pause for an app (`pauseForApps`) comes back as that app's pause, so leaving the app still ends it. (2) A window that got a kept place by its app alone holds it provisionally until the three minutes are over: a window with the saved title takes it over, and the first one floats where it opened. (3) After `resume` no place is kept for a window closed during the pause. (4) `status` says "1 place kept for a window that is not open yet"; `state` has a `waiting` list. (5) The second copy's alert says where Arbor is (the menu bar icon); the pid goes to the log only.
- App shell: if the window that holds a kept place provisionally closes during the wait, the place waits again (a welcome window often closes just before the real one opens), for no longer than it would have waited anyway. If the user has moved, floated or rearranged the holder meanwhile, nothing is taken over.
- App shell: at logout, restart and shutdown Arbor pauses for two minutes instead of quitting. Every window is in view before apps save their frames, macOS's own request to quit still ends Arbor, and a logout that some app cancels no longer leaves the session without Arbor. A pause someone asked for is not shortened.
- K1181: a window the saved arrangement does not know (it opened during a pause, or while Arbor was not running) is treated at `resume` and at launch as if Arbor had seen it open: with the Desktop out of view, framed or squeezed into a tile, it stays where it is, as a guest of that view (F57), or with `newWindowsWhileFramed` "float" until the view next moves. Floating windows the arrangement knows go out of view with the Desktop as before.
- K525: a window that comes back into sight after its app was excluded (Don't Arrange) gives up the place that waited for it under its number; it is neither moved nor given room.
- F62, second round (the product owner, 2026-10-14): the gap at the end of a kept group that lies in a row on its own axis resizes only the window on either side of it, as in a flat row; the same for `resize` and a window's own edge. What cannot be seen does not change what a drag does.
- F62, second round: a group the view or a saved view is on is kept firmly while a window is being moved, so a pair survives being rearranged.
- Core, second round: a weight above 1e12 in `state.json` is read as 1, like one that is not a positive number. What Arbor writes stays below 1e8.
- Core, second round: a boundary that arithmetic has left a hair (1e-6 of a point) below a half rounds up like a half, so that a window does not move by a point when weights change that do not concern it.
- K963: for half a second after a docked window comes back (out of the Dock, with its app, from another macOS Space or full screen) nothing is concluded from a frame it does not take: no minimum is learned, it is no misfit, no tile; its display is laid out once more when the half second is over. A window that really refuses becomes a tile that much later.
- F36: no `tileIcon` key. TrellisWM's `coverIcon: false` is F15's `"tileStyle": "plain"`; two keys for one thing could contradict each other.
- F36: `historyButtons` and `menuBarFill` are built with the menu bar buttons they belong to (F10), not here.
- F36: the tint is the accent colour at `tintBehindArranged` laid over the black at `dimBehindArranged`, in one fill; default 0 (TrellisWM's `tintOpacity` is 0.8, of nobody knows what).
- F36: the line around the Desktop is white, one pixel wide, just inside the Desktop's rectangle and rounded like a window. It is there only while the Desktop is in view as itself beside something docked: not when nothing is docked, not when the Desktop is a tile, framed or out of view.
- F36: with `"dimBehindArranged": 0` the line stays, because it belongs to the Desktop and not to the darkening; `"desktopEdge": 0` removes it.
- F36: `tileFade` is at most 1 second. A tile that is fading out takes no more clicks: what replaces it is under it already. Pausing and quitting do not wait for a fade.
- F12: before `reset` empties a list (`reset bindings`, `reset rules`, a list of apps) or everything, a copy of the file stays beside it as settings.before-reset.json, in place of an earlier one, and the reply names it; if no copy can be kept nothing is reset. A file that is replaced because it cannot be read is kept as settings.unreadable.json, as before, and the reply names that.
- F12 window: Restore All Shortcuts… and Replace It with the Defaults… ask first, in the place of the button, saying how much would go and where the copy stays. Leaving the pane is a no.
- F12: a word of caution for each row of "Never bind by default" in docs/KEYBOARD.md that names a hazard (the hyper key, Xcode's ⌃⌥⌘ + letter, ⌃⌥Q and ⌃⌥W, ⌃⌥⌫, Figma's and JetBrains' letters), none for ⌃⌥J and ⌃⌥K, which are left free for economy's sake. Nothing is refused.
- F12: replies about lists are in people's words, which the schema holds: "your 7 shortcuts and 3 rules are kept", "shortcuts and gestures are off in Mail".
- F12: an id that no app with a window open answers to is accepted, in a rule or on a list, with a word of caution: an app's name can have a dot in it too (zoom.us).
- F12: `set hotkeysEnabled false` ends with "Settings… in Arbor's menu is the way back".
- F12: the menu has no Launch at Login any more: the tick lives in the window (docs/MENU.md).
- F55, 2026-10-14, on the product owner's "the dominant axis at the start decides": a diagonal counts as up or down (zooming is what the keys are known for); nothing is decided until the scroll moves (some drivers begin one without movement); without `continuousZoom` there is nothing to slide, so every scroll with ⌃⌥ is a zoom however askew it starts; with `scrollZoom` off every one slides; a wheel turned sideways (⇧) is Arbor's and does nothing.
- F55, 2026-10-14 (the product owner, on the Input owner's remark): a scroll needs **exactly** ⌃⌥, as the rectangle gesture matches its modifiers exactly; ⌃⌥⌘ and the hyper key are not Arbor's for scrolling. ⇧ is let through for a wheel only. Decided with it: `scrollOnDesktopZooms` means without any modifier key; a key that goes down during a scroll of Arbor's changes nothing; the tap also wakes for ⌃⌥⇧, because what follows may be a wheel's notch.
- Performance review: windows are moved side by side through a hint (`moveTogether` foretells a layout's `setFrame` calls), not by rewriting `apply` into a batch. It costs one line in `relayout`, keeps the one-at-a-time code everybody is editing, and a window system may ignore it.
- F60, 2026-10-14 (coordinator): `guardDesktopClicks` is false by default until the guard has been seen at work on a Mac.
- F60, 2026-10-14: what may be on screen for a click to be kept back is a list of what is harmless (measured on a Mac at rest), not of what is not. So on a Mac with something unknown always up (a screen dimmer, a menu bar manager's overlay) the guard does nothing, which is the harmless way to fail.
